1989 after seeing a junk removal
truck in a McDonalds drivethrough lineup.

serious business messes to clean
up along the way, he began incorporating 1-800-GOTJUNK in 1999. lesson # 2 - 1 0 years for him to get to his
first decent wave of success... and it was only the beginning.
270 franchises by 2007! He has nowexpanded into the US
and Australia as well.
Lesson #3 - test systems out on your marketing - find ones
that work - make sure they are duplicabte - then go HOGWILD replicating them everywhere humanly possible.
One ad that works should be running in multiple locations,
cities, websites,; search engines. If it wprks in one place
consistently, it WILL work in others.
Lesson #4 - systems that work in one niche WILL work in
unrelated nichejs! Brian is a smart guy, so what does he do
next? Now that the junk business is screaming along and
making him a tidy bundle (all puns intended :), he looks
outside of the junk business to see what else fits his model.

Clean trucks. Clean movers, in uniform (and deodorant mandatory - again,

People LOVE IT. He does
what he does best - systematizes it - franchises it
- makes himself a mint
while moving mountains.

I'm not saying you need to start up unrelated businesses.
I AM saying you need to start thinking about what's working best in your business.
Right now. What's working for lead generation? Are your leads coming from online? Facebook? Linked in? Cold
-calling (stHI very effective - can be anyhow)? Offline?
Look at whafs working - then find ways to expand your best systems. New media. Newmarkets. New systems for follow up.
Next, look at simple ways to WOW your customers. How can you do bigger, more streamlined service/product
bundles that decrease another stress your client faces? Everyone has stresses. Some more than others. Your
products and services help them with one of more of these right now - they should at least. How can you make
it bigger, bolder, more grandiose?
The idea of painting in a day is a prime example. He brings a whole squadron of people in to knock it off in a
day. They pay a premium for it (considering its one DAY work)... but it is so worth it to be DONE WITH IT.
There is always a way to do this in your business!
THINK. Go away to somewhere quiet with pen and paper (by far the most effective way). Then write down all
the wacky ideas you can. How it related to stress your clients face. How you can do your service quicker, or
more streamlined. How you package your products, or bundle them, to take away more stresses than you do
now.
Then get it implemented. Test it. Run with it. Don't overanalyze it - which is what most people do. Good

any commissions they receive from other businesses are

Don't create - collaborate. Don't hesitate- reciprocate.

your ego out o f the w a y , and this is possible. Instead o f

strategy and synergy that sophisticated entrepreneurs

Two Things You Can Never Pay a Person to Do
And the REAL Job of an Entrepreneur

O n e o f t h e m o s t c o m m o n q u e s t i o n s t h a t w e all h e a r
o n a d a i l y b a s i s is: ' W h a t d o y o u d o ? "
F o r s o m e , t h i s is a n e a s y q u e s t i o n ; " I ' m a d o c t o r "
s o m e m a y reply; or "I'm a teacher" e v e r y o n e understands that doctors treat sick people and teachers
teach. For others, the questions "what do you do?" is
much m o r e abstract and hard to answer.

1) T h e y m u s t b e t h e E n t r e p r e n e u r , t h e v i s i o n a r y w h o
paints the broad strokes and has the energy and
drive to start the business.
2) T h e y m u s t be the Manager, the person w h o overs e e s t h e o p e r a t i o n a n d e n s u r e s t h a t it f u n c t i o n s e f f i ciently and sustainably.
3) T h e y m u s t be t h e T e c h n i c i a n a n d h a n d l e t h e daily
t r a n s a c t i o n a l w o r k in t h e b u s i n e s s .

In t h e p a s t , I h a d i n t r o d u c e d
myself as an "entrepreneur".
S o m e people say that
" e n t r e p r e n e u r " is a nice w o r d
for "unemployed" or
"unemployable". T h e problem
with being an entrepreneur is
t h a t is t h a t e n t r e p r e n e u r s h i p i s
so vast and abstract that m o s t
people cannot understand
what an entrepreneur does.
W h e n you say "I'm an entrepreneur", the c o m m o n reaction
is a b l a n k s t a r e , a n d t h e n t h e f o l l o w i n g q u e s t i o n " s o ,
what do you A C T U A L L Y do?"
In t r u t h , e n t r e p r e n e u r s a r e r e s p o n s i b l e f o r t h e e n t i r e
b u s i n e s s , o r multiple b u s i n e s s e s . T h e y hire, fire, delegate, select projects, start projects, finish projects,
m a n a g e a n d e v e n s o m e t i m e s w o r k in t h e b u s i n e s s .
However, the entrepreneur has 1job that only he o r
she can perform.
A great b u s i n e s s m a n once said; "there are only two
jobs that y o u cannot ever pay a person to do"; T h e s e
are the job of the entrepreneur - everything else can
be d o n e by s o m e o n e else.

A l l t a s k s in a n o r g a n i z a t i o n
c a n be delegated or hired
out to either a m a n a g e r or
employee and many organizations can run very efficiently o n mostly T e c h n i cians and Managers. However, the E n t r e p r e n e u r is
responsible for all of t h e
s t r a t e g i c t h i n k i n g in t h e
business and he or she
m u s t d e t e r m i n e t h e right
order of operations and
procedures.

'he "Business" of
Positioning Yourself
I recently attended a 2-day workshop on raising capital,
put on by Julie Broad and her husband, Dave Peniuk, from
RevNYou. It was ja very good event, and I picked up some
excellent pointers on raising money that I had never considered before. I had known of Julie and Dave for several
years and had heard lots of good things about them. That's
why I asked Julie; to come speak at my last R20 Summit,
and I liked her presentation so much, I bought her braining
event!
What does this have to do with 'business'? Lots, actually. You see, one of Ihe big 'takeaways' I got from the
event was all about how we position ourselves when approaching people for money. Now this even revolved
around raising money (and credit) from investors for buying
real estate, and the same principles apply no matter what
line of work you are in. As self-employed people, we are
always 'asking' people to invest... either to invest in our
products, invest in our services, or invest in our businesses
or properties. At fhe end of the day, it is all about getting
people to feel enough confidence in us that they are willing
to part with their money, in exchange for whateyer it is we are
offering.
However, for many of us, we go
about the 'positioning' of ourselves
and our products! or services completely wrong. We end up overiy
trying to please oiur potential customers / clients / investors, and it
almost seems like we are grovelling for their money.
Have you ever had someone
try to sell you something, and you
can feel their desperation for the
sale? This gives you an uneasy

feeling and actually may repel you from buying
anything from them. On the other hand, I'm sure
there have been times when you have bought
something from a person who really knew how to
position themselves. They were polite and helpful
- but they were not 'needy" (and, as our friend
Steve Chandler likes to say, 'needy is creepy").
They emitted a sensation of "I'd be doing you a
favor if I sold you this" to you, and that actually
attracted you more to the idea of buying.
So let's look at an example that Julie and
Dave demonstrated. They found that when investors are meeting with potential money partners,
they often get off to the wrong start right at the
beginning with their greeting. They often say
something like, "Hi, Mr. Smith, thank you so
much for your time today. I really appreciate you
meeting with me!"
Now, on the surface that may seem perfectly
acceptable and maybe even normal or
'expected'. But look at the positioning you have
when you do that - who is put up on the pedestal
as being more important or more valuable? Right
from the get-go, the potential money partner is
feeling like what they have
(money) is more valuable than
what you have to offer (your
product, service, property), and
that you are going to be 'selling'
them.
Compare that with the greeting Julie and Dave suggest instead: "Hi, Mr. Smitti, I'm happy
I to see you, and I'm glad I could
fit you in today. I've got another
1 appointment right after this, so
J we'll have to keep our meeting
!to45 minutes."

Julie Broad. D a v e D u b e a u . D a v e Peniuk.
The
p o w e r ofproper
positioning
(say that 4 times

profitable
quickly!)

from the first example. Here, you are coming across as a person who is busy and in demand -;and
that you are NOT desperate for their business.
Now you may be thinking, "Wow! That's a bit obnoxious, isn't it?" And that's what I thought at first
too - but it really isn't, as long as you say it pleasantly and with a smile and a firm handshake. |fs all

So the next time you are meeting with a client or a prospect, no matter what kind of business you are in - think
about your positioning. Are you putting yourself on equal
footing with the other person, or are you coming across as
'needy"?
Make yourself the 'prize' and you will actually make
more sales.

Do You Have an Elevator Pitch?

T h i s m o n t h , I s p o k e at a
w o m a n ' s e v e n t a n d f o u n d it
quite interesting that a f e w of
the w o m e n I spoke with were
selling m o r e t h a n o n e product at a t i m e . T h e y w e r e trying to give m e their e l e v a t o r
pitch o n m o r e t h a n o n e produ c t o r s e r v i c e , a n d it w a s
confusing to say the least. I
w a n t to share this with you
b e c a u s e it i s i m p o r t a n t . I f
you have more than one
business, you should have a
different pitch for e a c h business, and you should only
pitch o n e b u s i n e s s at a time.
If y o u r p i t c h b e c o m e s t o o
complicated and you are
o f f e r i n g t o o m a n y t h i n g s , it
will s i m p l y c o n f u s e y o u r
prospect. T e s t and market
y o u r pitches. If y o u a r e at a
n e t w o r k i n g event, pitch o n e
of your businesses, then at
the next event, pitch your
other business. Once you
h a v e c o m p l e t e d your pitch
a n d y o u r p r o s p e c t is interested and wants to know
m o r e a b o u t y o u , in a c o n v e r s a t i o n , y o u c a n t h e n tell
t h e m that you have more
than one business. W h e n
first m e e t i n g people, y o u
don't w a n t to appear to b e
d e s p e r a t e ; r e m e m b e r , it is all
about building rapport with
the other person and not
selling at t h e first point of
contact.
G e t in front of a s m a n y
people as you can and present as often as you can.
Always be ready with your
presentation because when
you are prepared, you can

say yes to last-minute
opportunities. I w a s attending a C o m p a n y of
W o m e n event last year
a n d o n e s p e a k e r didn't
s h o w up. S i n c e I had
s p o k e n at their e v e n t s in
the past, the o w n e r
c a m e up to m e and
a s k e d i f I c o u l d fill i n .
T h e r e a s o n I tell y o u
a b o u t t h i s is b e c a u s e
w h a t you are doing in
t h e s e m o m e n t s is selling. Selling w h a t ? y o u
a s k . S e l l i n g y o u ! T h a t is
what w e do w h e n w e are
out there prospecting
and networking. Getting
in front of a s m a n y p e o ple a s w e c a n a n d giving
o u r best. In t h e e n d , it
creates sales and business opportunities.
Here are a couple of
examples of elevator
pitches:
For the past 25 years, I
have helped thousands
of women earn millions
of dollars in direct sales.
Is that something that
you would be interested
in?

Hi, my name is
with
. We offer a 100%
money-back guarantee
to profitably increase
sales and build a championship team in any
company. We do this
through a combination of
education, inspiration,
and accountability. One
company we recently
worked with increased
their sales more than
38% in less than six
weeks by using our sim-

ple system. Would you
like to know more?

My company, Pegasus
School Services, works
with Secondary Principals and VPs like you to
help them increase discretionary funds from
the services they are
already buying. Instead
of buying fall, graduation and event photography, yearbooks, motivational banners, spirit
wear, grad wear and
caps and gowns
through numerous vendors, our clients receive
easy to manage bundled products and services from Pegasus that
provide greater value
for students, parents
and staff. What services
do you presently use?
Success can be simple. G i v e c u s t o m e r s
what they want, and
t h e y ' l l c o m e b a c k . If y o u
provide great service
a n d m a k e it a g o a l t o
satisfy your customers,
t h e y will k e e p c o m i n g

b a c k a n d will r e c o m m e n d you to others.
Y o u r r o l e is t o s e r v e
your customers and
give t h e m what they
w a n t . If y o u d o n ' t a l w a y s r e m e m b e r this,
s o m e o n e else will remember, and turn your
c u s t o m e r into their c u s tomer.
Run your business
with enthusiasm, positive energy, and optim i s m , a n d g e t rid o f t h e
negativity that m a y be
holding you back. Build
a t e a m around y o u that
will inspire y o u t o greatn e s s . T h e r e is a bigger/
better person inside of
each and every one of
u s . G i v e y o u r s e l f a gift
today and go after what
y o u w a n t ; it i s e a s i e r
t h a n y o u think!

Can We Skip All This Marketing and Go Get Customers?
A t e l e p h o n e call. A busin e s s o w n e r in great pain.
A s k i n g if I c a n help.
"Wassup?" Iasked. "We're
going d o w n , " h e replied. A n d
h e w e n t o n to tell m e his
story - which I a m delighted
to share with you, m y fellow
Canadian Business Profiteers.
This company makes mobility p r o d u c t s f o r t h e s e n i o r s
market. O v e r 6 0 % of their
sales c a m e out of Ontario.
W h y s o h e a v y in o n e a r e a ?
Because the government
subsidizes these products.
But, the g o v e r n m e n t suddenly lowered the m a x i m u m
price of the mobility products
that they will subsidize. T h e
result? O u r caller's Ontario
mobility sales w e n t from
sixty to z e r o , i n n o t i m e flat.
This (obviously) w a s a
genuine catastrophe. He
went o n to say that they
can't lower their prices to
meet the lower government
subsidy ceiling "because w e
manufacture a top quality
p r o d u c t m a d e totally in C a n ada." ("Hmm," I thought.
" W i t h tight quality control y o u
can m a k e a darn good product o v e r s e a s t h e s e days."
But that's another story.)
So. W h a t to do? H e said
they w e r e reviewing their
options, including shutting
down the company. "Ouch," I
said. " Y o u would shut d o w n
just because a government
c h a n g e s its policy?" (I e n courage m y clients " n e v a h "
to depend on a government
program of a n y kind " e v a h . " B u t t h a t t o o is a n other story.) I asked where
the other 4 0 % of his sales
are made.

" M o s t l y in Florida,"
h e replied.
"In Florida?" I echoed. "That's the hottest
seniors market on the
continent - m a y b e on
the planet. W h y don't
you expand your sales
t h e r e - a n d right a c r o s s
the S u n Belt - the w h o l e
American South and
West?"

"We'd love to," he
said, "but w e ' r e broke
and w e don't have either
the time or the m o n e y to
d o all t h e m a r k e t i n g a n d
s a l e s d e v e l o p m e n t it
would take. W e ' d have
to go into trade s h o w s ;
find distributors; train
their sales people there's just no time, even
if w e h a d t h e m o n e y . "
M y reply: " W h y don't
y o u s k i p all t h a t m a r k e t ing a n d sales stuff - and
just go down there and
get s o m e c u s t o m e r s ? "
O u r caller w a s silent.
F o r a long time. Finally,
he asked, "How would
you do that?" And I want
t o tell y o u w h a t I told h i m
- w h i c h i s t h i s : W h e n I'm
doing turnarounds with
c o m p a n i e s in crisis, w e
never have time to do a
w h o l e lot o f " b u s i n e s s
development." Instead,
w e ' v e simply got to gen-

e r a t e s a l e s - right n o w !
A n d that's what I urged
him to do.
I suggested that he
use an approach that I
call "introduction m a r keting" (or, interchangeably,
"introduction selling").
All h e h a d t o d o w a s call
h i s c l i e n t s in F l o r i d a
(stores that sell mobility
equipment and supplies
t o s e n i o r s ) a n d tell t h e m
he's coming down for a
month. Line up appointments with each o f
t h e m for golf, dinner,
fishing, whatever. T h e n
ask each one who,
a m o n g their fellow ( n o n competing) dealers, h e
should be talking to and doing business with
- in t h e r e g i o n . A n d
then ask t h e m for
"personal introductions"
to these n e w prospective dealers.
Notice that I didn't
suggest that he ask for
"referrals." In m y o p i n ion, referrals are not
enough - because
w h e n y o u call a
stranger (even w h e n
you've been referred)
you can expect a "cool"
reception. This m a k e s a
r e f e r r a l call a " c o o l c a l l "
i n m y b o o k s . It m a y n o t

bad a s a "cold call" (I
a m totally o p p o s e d t o
c o l d c a l l i n g ) , b u t it i s n ' t
as good as a "warm
call," a n d that's t h e o n l y
k i n d o f call I w a n t m y
sales training clients to
make.
I explained the "cold,
cool, w a r m " aspect of
calling and urged h i m t o
remind his dealers h o w
a w k w a r d it is t o m a k e
and receive unsolicited
sales calls - a n d that's
w h y h e is a s k i n g t h e m
to call their dealer
friends - and fully introduce him - before he
called t h e m . E v e n better, I s u g g e s t e d that h e
ask t h e m to line up
lunch meetings with
these prospects - so
that all t h r e e a r e a t t h e
table for a truly
"personal" introduction.
Is t h e r e a r i s k i n t a k ing this approach? O f
c o u r s e . W h a t if his Florida d e a l e r s a r e l u k e w a r m about the

WHY

DISRUPTIONS A R E E S S E N T I A L TO ACHIEVING Y O U R G O A L S

The
I other day, I
|was coaching o n e of
our members whose ambit i o n is t o b e c o m e financially
independent through real
estate investing within the
next five years. H e w a s feeling d i s c o u r a g e d a n d d o w n
on himself because h e is
m a k i n g v e r y little h e a d w a y
t o w a r d s his goal.
A s h e spoke the details,
frustration o f his situation
began t oemerge. W h e n h e
came t ome, he w a s in a mid
-level corporate position that
w a s keeping him busy.
Shortly thereafter, h e w a s
promoted toa more dem a n d i n g role in a different
office that not only added t o
his responsibilities but also
increased his c o m m u t e . W i t h
a young family and a house
to look after, he w a s struggling to find time to w o r k tow a r d s his d r e a m . H e told m e
that he frequently plans to d o
real e s t a t e projects in the
e v e n i n g s b u t h e is j u s t t o o
t i r e d . H e a l s o f i n d s it difficult
to be productive o n weeke n d s g i v e n all t h e kids activities and family pressures.
I h a v e had this conversation m a n y times before with
clients w h o w e r e trying to get
a h e a d . T h e i r current reality
s e e m s to be an obstacle t o
creating the future they really
w a n t . W h a t is t h e s o l u t i o n t o
this apparent trap?
M y first q u e s t i o n i s
w h e t h e r h i s g o a l is a c h i e v able. T o m e , almost a n y goal
is a c h i e v a b l e . T h e o n l y
q u e s t i o n is w h e t h e r y o u a r e
w i l l i n g t o d o w h a t it t a k e s t o
a c h i e v e it. T h e b i g g e r t h e

Page 1 2

goal, the m o r e radical
the action required t o
m a k e it h a p p e n .
In t h i s s p e c i f i c c a s e , I
k n o w his objective is
achievable because I
h a v e dealt with m a n y
real estate investors
over the years. I have
watched a t least four of
t h e m start our p r o g r a m
with a net worth close t o
zero and build multimillion dollar real estate
portfolios in less t h a n
three years. If o t h e r s
h a v e d o n e it, h e c a n t o o .
So what's stopping
h i m ? A s w e t a l k e d , it
b e c a m e crystal clear that
h e really d o e s h a v e a
desire to achieve his
g o a l , b u t h e is t r y i n g t o
d o it w i t h o u t d i s r u p t i n g
h i s life. H e w a n t s t o f i n d ,
purchase, finance, and
operate adozen or more
properties in a short per i o d o f t i m e a n d still k e e p
everything he has today.
H e is not prepared t o
compromise anything h e
has now, and he doesn't
want to upset anyone
else either. Clearly that
is n o t g o i n g t o h a p p e n ,
but d o n ' t w e all try to d o
the s a m e thing?
H e r e is t h e b r e a k t h r o u g h point. B e c o m i n g
successful must, b y necessity, disrupt your
s t a t u s q u o ! If y o u k e e p
doing what you are doing, y o u will s t a y w h e r e
you are.
Think for a m o m e n t
about s o m e o n e w h o has
achieved considerable
s u c c e s s . Did t h e y d o it
without significantly al-

tering their life? N o w a y
- it c a n ' t b e d o n e ! A l l o f
m y clients w h o built significant real e s t a t e portfolios turned their lives
upside d o w n inorder to
achieve their goals. In
fact, achieving their goal
b e c a m e the biggest prio r i t y in t h e i r l i f e , a n d
t h e y fit e v e r y t h i n g e l s e
a r o u n d it.
An example. One of
our long-time members
in A l b e r t a i s c u r r e n t l y
training for the Ultraman
Triathalon taking place
in H a w a i i t h i s y e a r . Y e s ,
t h a t is a 6 - m i l e o p e n
water swim and a 260mile bike race, followed
by a 52-mile double
m a r a t h o n . His training
regime takes up to 2 5
hours a week. He does
this while h e o w n s and
manages agym with 2 7
staff and 1 0 0 0 m e m bers, plus he spends
time with his young family. D o y o u t h i n k t h i s i s
disrupting his life? I
asked him how he mana g e d it, a n d h e s a i d ,
"Sure, the training is
inconvenient, but I w a n t
to p e r f o r m w e l l in t h a t
race. W h e n something
is i m p o r t a n t e n o u g h ,
y o u j u s t fit it i n . "

Inside this m e s s a g e ,
there is g o o d n e w s and
bad news. T h e bad n e w s
i s t h a t if y o u w a n t t o
a c h i e v e s o m e t h i n g signific a n t , y o u m u s t m a k e it
y o u r n u m b e r o n e priority.
A n d it i s g u a r a n t e e d t o
disrupt y o u r life.
T h e g o o d n e w s is that
w e h u m a n beings are
amazingly adaptable. Although w e naturally avoid
disruption, w e very quickly
settle into n e w situations
w h e n required. W h e n you
m o v e to a n e w city, h o m e ,
or job, your entire routine
c h a n g e s . A t first, y o u just
w a n t t o "get life b a c k t o
normal." However, within
a short time, you do adjust, and a n e w normal is
established.
T h e key to success is
t o d e c i d e w h a t is really
important to you and
m a k e that your number
o n e priority. T a k e action
o n it a n d fit t h e r e s t o f
y o u r life a r o u n d it. It m a y
t a k e s o m e adjusting, but
within a short time, a n e w
routine will e m e r g e ; o n e
that will e n s u r e y o u really
do achieve your goals and
dreams.

Live Auctions and Online Auctions
Today, w e are going t o
t a l k a bit a b o u t e x p a n d i n g
your online sales business.
I h a v e t a l k e d a bit a b o u t
this in t h e past and told
everyone h o w m y partner
Jon has opened up a
physical consignment
s t o r e t o w o r k right a l o n g side his online e B a y sales
business. N o w I a m going
to talk about expanding
e v e n further. A l s o in t h e
past, I h a v e talked about
sourcing merchandise
f r o m local auctions w h e r e
you can get screaming
good deals on almost any
sort of merchandise. T o d a y ' s article i s g o i n g t o roll
all o f t h a t i n t o a s i n g l e c o n cept. T h a t c o n c e p t is t o
expand your online auction
b u s i n e s s w i t h a real live
auction business. I have
done just that, and I a m
pleased to a n n o u n c e that
Buckaru Auctions is n o w a
live auction b u s i n e s s a n d
w e are able to hold and
h o s t live a u c t i o n s in addition t o our online sales.
F o r t h e l a s t little w h i l e ,
both m y partner Jon and
myself have been attending auction school t o learn
h o w to d o live in p e r s o n
auctions, a n d just last
week, w e graduated and
are n o w fully accredited
live a u c t i o n e e r s . W e h o p e
t o b e holding o u r first live
auction sometime very
s o o n in t h e E d m o n t o n
area.
B y adding a live auction
component to your busin e s s , y o u will actually
capitalize o n a n u m b e r o f
different a r e a s . First of all,
y o u will n o w h a v e a n outlet
to dispose of the merchandise that is not appropriate

to sell online though eBay,
such as furniture and comm o n household items, a s
well as low-value items.
Next, y o u will a l s o h a v e
people bringing you items
to sell. A s m o s t o f y o u
know, Ihave always encouraged the concept of
consignment selling as a
great w a y to source merchandise to sell. W i t h a
live a u c t i o n , p e o p l e will b e
bringing y o u their merchandise t o sell in droves.
Y o u can n o w sell their
merchandise online o r
through
the live
auction, o r
even
through
your
s t o r e if
you
have
expanded
that w a y . H a v i n g a live
auction i s literally a license
for people to bring you
their stuff to sell.

Twitter Writing Tips

11
Writing on Twitter is by
necessity an exercise in brevity, and it may surprise you to
find out that B2B marketing
can actually be quite effective
even when you can use a
maximum of only 120 characters.

3
8

Here is an important tip:
just stick to the facts. Long
titles may be S E O friendly,
but more than likely they will
just get lost on Twitter, where
they simply do not work. Titles need to be short, to the
point and appealing.

8

7 3
8

1 4
5

7

6 5

1

On Twitter it is not a bad
thing to be familiar; messages with a famous quote, a
hit song or a piece of pop culture often resonate. Tweets
that carry with them some
kind of positive connotation
will give your message extra
impact. This does not mean,
however, that you can just
copy text outright, so exercise some discretion.

Being timely is also important on Twitter. You are

needs to be in tandem with

who hesitates on Twitter is
truly lost.

inefficient companies did create substantial value, but that value did not
flow to the now laid-off workers. Some might flow to the remaining workers, but much of it went to the engineers who restructured the companies
and the investors they represented. USA Statistics reveal that, since
1947 (when the data was first compiled), corporate profits as a percentage of gross domestic product are now at their highest level, while wages
as a percentage of GDP are now at their lowest level. It was not a question of making the economy more efficient... it did do that... it was a
question of where the value accumulated. The upper segment of the
wage curve and the investors continued to make money. The middle
class divided into a segment that entered the upper-middle class, while
another faction sank into the lower-middle class.

For Canadian Business Owners, Entrepreneurs and Self-Employed Professionals

August Smoggest...
A Simple Way to Cut Through
the Fog and Clear the Path to
Bigger Profits
With Tory White
Hope you are having a FANTASTIC summer so far!
Time flies fast, and before we know it... CHRISTMAS will
be here! Which also means your business and personal
goals must be on the right track by now, or you need a serious reboot if you are going to make it happen.
A big part of success in marketing and business is in the
stories you tell your prospects and clients. No better way to
win some brownie points than sharing some personal
things going on in your life. I've tested using personal stories in business to business AND businesstoconsumer BOTH WORK. Actually B2B works even better because NO
ONE does it! I did a campaign for a client a while back that
was targeted at the purchasing managers of $100 million
dollar type companies. Busy guys. Lots of sales people
trying to win them over.
We didn't know a soul. A cold list of 200 of these managers - some of them even owners of the company. The
letter I wrote talked about family, kids, dreams and aspirations. NOTHING like your typical 'pitch' letter or brochure.
Get this... 19% of them responded to the letter! Not just
responded - but THANKED US for writing such a personal
letter. Try that with a brochure!
Stories are your secret weapon in 2013 and beyond.
USE THEM.
Here's one for you... I have kept certain parts of my personal life pretty quiet over the past 19 months. Rare for me
- but some major things happened that I wasn't comfortable
sharing. Why share them now? Because the past 19

months taught me a ton of powerful things about
improving myself, and my business... and I would
like to pass them on to you.
Eariy January 2012,1 was fed up. Fed up with
me. Fed up with my health. Fed up with the direction my personal life was going. And fed up with
certain parts of my business. Have you been
there? We all have. I did something about it
though. DRASTIC action.
I ended up divorced after 12 years of marriage. That was very difficult - not something I
wish on anyone. But change was needed. Fast
forward 19 months and everyone is doing fine,
new lives have begun, and positive changes
came as a result.
MORE drastic change... my health. I have
always been active in martial arts and walking,
but it wasn't enough. I carried far too much extra
weight for the exercise I was getting, I didn't feel
that great, and I had lost my self-confidence...
ultimately leading to depression. So I did something about it. Fast forward 19 months and I
dropped ~30 pounds in fat and put on ~20
pounds in muscle. I feel great and my selfconfidence renewed.
My business was another area where I wanted CHANGE, massive change. Fast forward 19
months, and I have redesigned much of my business and some MASSIVE new developments are
just about to be announced in the next month.
WHAT DOES THIS HAVE TO DO WITH
YOU?
3 Cs. The same 3 Cs I now abide by. The
same 3 Cs that changed the direction my life was
headed in. The same 3 Cs that can help YOU
change what you need to change.

T R A C K T O E B A Y BY LARRY YAKIWCZUK

A Hodge Podge of Ebay Topics

Hi there. This month's
a r t i c l e w i l l b e a bit o f a
hodge podge and miscellaneous ramblings on a
n u m b e r of different topics.
O n e thing I w o u l d like
t o d i s c u s s is w h a t t o d o if
the item you receive is not
e x a c t l y w h a t y o u t h o u g h t it
was. This is actually a
c o m m o n problem and occurs m o r e than you would
think. Generally t h e s e type
of problems are quite m i nor, but a n n o y i n g . A class i c e x a m p l e is t h a t t h e
i t e m y o u g e t is a s l i g h t l y
different color t h a n w h a t
the photo was. This can
occur for a n u m b e r of reasons, and n o o n e is really
to b l a m e . C o l o r is a v e r y
subjective thing, and even
though one person describes a particular color,
another person would des c r i b e it t o t a l l y d i f f e r e n t l y .
A l s o , all digital c a m e r a s
record colors slightly differently, a n d m o n i t o r s m a y
display c o l o r s slightly differently. S o there is n o real
w a y to accurately depict
the true color of an item.
This is a case w h e r e close
actually d o e s count. If colo r is r e a l l y i m p o r t a n t , t h e n
you should ask s o m e
questions before bidding
on the item and confirm
exactly w h a t the true color
is c o m p a r e d t o s o m e t h i n g
of a k n o w n color. A l s o ,
m a k e it c l e a r t o t h e s e l l e r
t h a t c o l o r is a n i m p o r t a n t
p o i n t , a n d a s k if t h e i t e m
m a y b e r e t u r n e d i f it is n o t
t h e c o l o r y o u t h o u g h t it
w a s . T h e seller m a y o r
m a y not allow you t o return

Page 6

the item. Don't get too ups e t if t h e s e l l e r d o e s n o t
want the hassle and asks
you to purchase elsewhere.
If y o u d o g e t a n i t e m
that's different, t h e first
s t e p is t o g o b a c k to t h e
l i s t i n g a n d c o m p a r e it t o
w h a t w a s listed. T h e n politely a s k t h e s e l l e r if a r e turn w o u l d be a possibility.
R e m e m b e r that politeness
r e a l l y d o e s c o u n t in a s i t u ation like this. Being forceful a n d o b n o x i o u s will o n l y
result in b a d f e e d b a c k a n d
not being able to return the
item. I suggest complimenting the seller on the
item you received and say
that you are sure that he
described the item as best
h e could, but y o u h a v e a
p r o b l e m w i t h it, a n d y o u
w o u l d l i k e t o r e t u r n it. I f
possible, a l w a y s allow the
other person a w a y to s a v e
face.
I recently purchased a n
item that turned out to be a
fake. R a t h e r than getting
a l l u p s e t w i t h it, I d e c i d e d
to do s o m e research and
play the g a m e . I found o u t
that the seller had a previo u s reputation for selling
fakes (yes, I should have
d o n e t h a t b e f o r e I bid!) a n d
also refused to take returns. I contacted the seller
and thanked h i m for the
prompt shipping of the
item. Ithen mentioned that
there w a s a slight problem
w i t h t h e i t e m a n d if I c o u l d
d i s c u s s it w i t h h i m . W h e n
he indicated w e could discuss the situation, I again
thanked him for responding a n d t h e n g a v e h i m d e tailed r e a s o n s w h y I think
the item he shipped m e

w a s a f a k e . I t h e n told

Understanding the Sales Promotion Law
r e s u l t in s e r i o u s r e p e r cussions, including cessation o f activity.
A r e y o u selling into the
United S a t e s ? If s o , y o u r
sales promotion strategies
m u s t not fall a f o u l o f U S
law. Just a s k M a r c E m e r y ,
"The Prince o f Pot," what
happens w h e n you do.
Sales promotion includes multiple promotional activities that a i m t o
convince customers to
purchase specific goods
and services. T h e s e activities typically relate t o
quantity offers (2+1) o r
value offers (discounts).
T o simplify things, marketing e x p e r t s h a v e d i v i d e d
sales promotions into t w o
main categories: businessoriented and consumeroriented, including trade,
industrial, and business
promotions on o n e side,
a n d retail a n d c o n s u m e r
promotions o n the other.
Regardless o fthe category
a sales p r o m o t i o n falls
u n d e r , it i s a l w a y s u s e d t o
lift s a l e s , c l e a r o u t i n v e n t o ries, attract c u s t o m e r s , o r
introduce a n e w product o r
service.
Sales Promotion Principles: T h e F e d e r a l T r a d e
Commission (FTC) uses
the ICC Consolidated
Code t o regulate marketing a n d a d v e r t i s i n g activities, including s a l e s promotions. According t o this
commission, the business
owners using sales promotion strategies to boost
their r e v e n u e m u s t comply
with a particular set o f
rules. Failure t o d o s o will

According tothe ICC
Consolidated Code, all
sales promotions must:
• reflect the product o r
service accurately,
w i t h o u t glamorizing it
• s h o w responsibility,
meeting consumers'
expectations
• contain transparent
c o n t e n t that will not
cause offense to the
consumers
Additionally, promoters m u s t m a k e sure that
t h e y a r e a b l e t o fulfill a l l
the obligations arising
from sales promotions
efficiently a n d promptly.
Sales Promotion
Administration: P r o moters must use adequate resources to m a n age sales promotions.
Particularly, they have
the obligation to:
• notify c o n s u m e r s
about promotions
• ensure the items they
promote meet the demands and express
terms o f offers
• replace inadequate
services and defective
goods with appropriate o n e s o r provide
financial c o m p e n s a tion
• handle all complaints
efficiently
Considering these
points, business o w n e r s
m u s t find a w a y to apply

the best sales promotion practices t o
demonstrate that the
main purpose of these
p r o m o t i o n s is t o m e e t
customers' expectations,
and not to disappoint
them.
Free Offers: T h e
term "free" defines a n
offer that involves n o
obligations. Although
s o m e free offers m a y
require customers t o
cover handling and shipping charges, which is
completely legal, the
Consolidated ICC Code
specifies that t h e s e offers should never ask a
c o n s u m e r to cover additional costs, such as the
costs o f returns. Moreover, the warranties that
usually accompany free
offers m u s t include particular limitations o n c o n s u m e r r i g h t s , if a p p l i c a ble, a n d provide clear
information about the
guarantor, including
name, address, and
phone number. W h e n
presenting a n offer, promoters have the responsibility t o e x p l a i n all p o tential obligations before
c o n s u m e r s accept the
respective offer.
Prize Promotion
Regulations: A p r i z e
promotion must provide
complete information

relevant to c o n s u m e r
interests. Information
should include details
regarding participation
criteria; a s s o c i a t e d
costs; nature, value,
and n u m b e r o f prizes;
procedure for a w a r d ing t h e prize; t a x information; and closing
date. P r o m o t e r s m u s t
also m a k e a clear distinction b e t w e e n a gift
and a prize.
For media-led promotions, businesses
have the obligation t o
design and present
materials that people
can easily distinguish
f r o m other editorial
components. Additionally, t h e p r o m o t i o n s
that will benefit a n o n profit s h o u l d n a m e t h e
respective organization and d e m o n s t r a t e
that the cause consents tothe promotion.
T o improve the
sales promotion experience for all t h e parties involved, the F T C
w o r k s diligently t o e n s u r e t h a t all p r o m o t e r s
comply with the current regulatory requirements.

An Ant's Determination
There are people w h o

I w a s gardening the other

A n t s n e v e r quit! T h a t is a

philosophy to sales; never

k e e p o n going like there is

all w e p o s s i b l y c a n ?
S u m m e r tends to slow
us d o w n and w e don't
prepare for winter w h e n
it g e t s b u s y . W e c a n ' t b e

s u m m e r just
as much as
w e d o in t h e
\^
winter s o w e
don't starve.
It i s i m p o r t a n t
to think
ahead and
p l a n ; if w e

be headed for a storm.
H i s t o r y is c h o c k full o f

great accomplishments.
Benjamin Franklin w a s
told b y experts t o l e a v e
a l o n e all t h a t f o o l i s h e x p e r i m e n t i n g w i t h lightn i n g . T h e y s a i d it w a s a
waste of time. Fortunately, h e d i d n o t q u i t ; h e
w a s persistent.
Henry Ford was
urged by T h o m a s Edison
to a b a n d o n his w o r k o n
t h e i d e a o f a m o t o r car.
"It i s a w o r t h l e s s i d e a , "
remarked Edison. Henry
F o r d w o u l d n o t quit; h e
w a s persistent.
Orville and Wilbur
Wright were spending a
lot o f t i m e a n d e n e r g y o n
s o m e t h i n g that w o u l d fly
i n t o t h e air. I n 1 9 0 8 , o n a
field i n Kitty H a w k , N C ,
the brothers taxied their
idea d o w n a h o m e m a d e
runway and launched u s
i n t o t h e air. O r v i l l e a n d

W i l b u r w o u l d not quit;

A b r a h a m Lincoln r a n
for legislature a n d
failed. H e entered busin e s s and failed, s p e n d ing 1 7 y e a r s paying o f f
his debts. H e ran f o r

Lower Loon at Last

T h e C a n a d i a n dollar
has been on a steady
decline which, with the
possible exception o f
cross-border shoppers,
should be w e l c o m e news.
T h e L o o n i e last traded a t
par with the U S dollar last
February and has since
b e e n t r a d i n g in a d i s t i n c t
pattern of decline with
little e v i d e n c e o f a r e v e r sal. In April a n d a g a i n i n
early May, the Canadian
dollar m a d e a run for par
but fell s h o r t a t . 9 9 8 ( o n
M a y 8 ) before sliding
back down.
t h

P a r w a s clearly the
resistance point before
the Loonie's support level
of 0.969 w a s broken on
May 21 , which became
the n e w resistance level
by July.
s t

F r o m the beginning o f
March to the end of June,
t h e d o l l a r t r a d e d in a n
identifiable pattern called
a "head and shoulder".
T h i s pattern is c h a r a c t e r ized by a n initial rise a n d
fall (first s h o u l d e r ) , followed by a second, larger
rise a n d fall ( h e a d ) a n d a
third lesser b u m p (second
shoulder). T h e predicted
m o v e m e n t after the patt e r n f o r m a t i o n is c o n t i n -

ued decline.

growth.

T h e n e w resistance level
(0.969) has since been tested
t h r e e t i m e s o f a r in J u l y o n t h e
10 , 15 and 18 w h e n the
dollar reached 0.965, 0.964
a n d 0 . 9 6 5 r e s p e c t i v e l y . If t h e
d o l l a r is a b l e t o s u r g e u p w a r d
b e t w e e n w h e n this article w a s
written and the end of July,
then there m a y be hope that
t h e L o o n will again challenge
t h e U S dollar. It is, h o w e v e r ,
unlikely.

W h i l e C a n a d a ' s trade deficit s h r a n k nearly
7 0 % , it is w i d e l y a c c e p t e d t h a t it w a s d u e t o i m ports shrinking faster than exports. Just s o w e
a r e a l l c l e a r , t h e t r a d e d e f i c i t is t h e d i f f e r e n c e
between h o w m u c h w e import a n d export. T h e
h i g h e r t h e deficit, t h e m o r e w e i m p o r t v e r s u s
e x p o r t . In o t h e r w o r d s , w e a r e s p e n d i n g m o r e
m o n e y t h a n e a r n i n g . A s h r i n k i n g d e f i c i t is g e n e r a l l y a g o o d s i g n , b u t in t h i s c a s e , it is a r e s u l t o f
trade dropping on both sides. Exporters would
be glad to get a n y help t h e y c a n get (i.e. a l o w e r
dollar).

t h

t h

t h

T h e U S e c o n o m y is p i c k i n g
up s t e a m with r u m o u r s of their
easing of quantitative easing,
improving e m p l o y m e n t figures,
surging housing prices and
general A m e r i c a n bravado. All

g r e e n b a c k , w h i c h if all e l s e

with.
A l o w e r C a n a d i a n dollar,
h o w e v e r , is w e l c o m e n e w s ,
particularly for our exporters
w h o have grave concerns
about China. China became
C a n a d a ' s second largest trading partner o v e r t h e past f e w
years, but their spectacular
growth is slowing d o w n . China's d e m a n d for Canadian
lumber and other resources
could drop considerably. A
w e a k e r dollar, h o w e v e r , m a y
help maintain China's interest
in C a n a d i a n g o o d s d e s p i t e
t h e i r s l o w e r , y e t still d e c e n t

C a n a d a ' s provincial and national debt, which
t o p p e d 1.3 t r i l l i o n c o m b i n e d i n M a y , a s w e l l a s
o u r deficit, w h i c h a d d e d 4 2 b i l l i o n t o o u r d e b t ,
will a l s o be e a s i e r to m a n a g e with a w e a k e r dollar. T h e c o s t t o r e f i n a n c e t h a t d e b t , i.e. i s s u i n g
m o r e bonds, would cost relatively less.
A l o w e r dollar will a l s o give the B a n k of C a n a d a t h e f r e e d o m t o r a i s e i n t e r e s t r a t e s if all o t h e r f a c t o r s w a r r a n t it. C a n a d a , a l o n g w i t h m u c h
of the world, h a s been surviving o n historically
l o w r a t e s in a n a t t e m p t t o s p u r s p e n d i n g a n d
economic growth. Raising the interest rate
would give our central bank m o r e firepower to
deal with future economic crises as well as dev e l o p c o n f i d e n c e in o u r e c o n o m y .
It i s n o t a l l r o s y n e w s , o f c o u r s e . O t h e r t h a n
t h e h i g h e r c o s t t o s h o p in t h e U S , t h e c o s t f o r
imported g o o d s should rise, and inflation could
b e c o m e a problem. T h a t being said, inflation is
still o n t h e l o w e n d o f t h e t a r g e t e d s p e c t r u m , s o
w e h a v e r o o m to m o v e .
I'm not a f a n o f a 6 5 - c e n t dollar, but I a l s o
d o n ' t m i n d if w e a r e n o t c o n s t a n t l y fighting t o
s t a y a t par. M a n y e c o n o m i s t s a r e e x p e c t i n g t h e
C a n a d i a n dollar to hold or at least not drop c o n s i d e r a b l y m o r e , s o t h e r e i s still t i m e t o e n j o y t h e
benefits of a stronger dollar.

T U R N A R O U N D M A R K E T I N G WITH N E I L G O D I N

"It's the Last Sustainable Competitive Advantage!"
T h i s m o n t h , I w a n t to
share the approach I use in
coaching business owners. I
call t h e a p p r o a c h "the A B C s
o f s u c c e s s , " a n d it g o e s l i k e
this: A. Deliver exceptional
c u s t o m e r s e r v i c e . B. D e v e l op a marketing strategy that
gets ideal prospective cust o m e r s coming to you. C .
Build sophisticated sales
skills that e n a b l e y o u to c o n vert the great majority of ideal prospects into long t e r m
customers.
If y o u l o o k c a r e f u l l y a t t h i s
model, you m a y notice that
delivering exceptional customer service c o m e s
"before" marketing and selling. T h a t m a y s e e m counterintuitive... don't y o u h a v e to
b r i n g c u s t o m e r s in b e f o r e
y o u can s e r v e t h e m ? But, for
the next few minutes, I want
to s h a r e t h e logic behind
putting s e r v i c e first - w h i l e
inviting y o u to l o o k at h o w
you could apply this approach to your business.
R e a d y ? Here w e go.
I place service first bec a u s e it's l i k e l a y i n g t h e
foundation for success. If
your entire business is predicated o n delivering genuinely e x c e p t i o n a l c u s t o m e r s e r v i c e - if t h a t ' s y o u r " b r a n d
p r o m i s e " - a n d if y o u t a k e
the steps necessary to deliver on your promise - then
y o u will e n j o y t h e last s u s tainable competitive adv a n t a g e in b u s i n e s s .
W h y do I call e x c e p t i o n a l
c u s t o m e r service the last
sustainable competitive advantage? Because anything
else you do can be copied
by competitors. O n the other
hand, genuinely exceptional
service can be seen, heard

a n d f e l t - b u t it c a n ' t b e
c o p i e d . W h y c a n ' t it b e
c o p i e d ? B e c a u s e it r e quires exceptionally high
levels of h u m a n perform a n c e - in s e l f m a n a g e m e n t , selfresponsibility, interpersonal communication,
leadership, teamwork,
creative and lateral
thinking, and problem
solving.
Here's an example. I
a s k e v e r y n e w client to

hate it when I spend

frustrated, confused,

d o e s "not" set the stage
for great customer service. Instead, harboring
these negative feelings
and sharing t h e m with

feel that customers take
a d v a n t a g e o f u s in d i f f e r ent ways. This "blame

of genuinely exceptional

H o w do w e deal with

avoid playing "the victim
g a m e . " W e play this
game any time we
n a m e a "villain," b y

Problem: The moment
w e appoint s o m e o n e as

ourselves as their
"victim." A n d the probl e m w i t h t h a t is t h i s : T h e
j o b o f a v i c t i m is t o s u f fer at the h a n d s of the

a c t u a l p r o b l e m . In f a c t ,
the best a victim can
h o p e f o r is a " h e r o " w h o
will lend a s y m p a t h e t i c
ear w h e n he complains
a b o u t t h e villain. H o w
do w e end this g a m e ?
B y refusing to play - by
refusing to n a m e and
b l a m e a villain. By d e ciding that y o u are n o
o n e ' s p o w e r l e s s little
victim - that w h e n y o u

situation), y o u choose

the blame game.

in w h i c h e v e r y i n d i v i d u a l is c o m m i t t e d t o b e h a v i n g in t h i s w a y . . . t o
taking personal responsibility f o r t h e s i t u a t i o n s
you're in... and getting
t h e benefit o f h a v i n g all

that would usually b e
w a s t e d in t h e b l a m e
g a m e available for
brainstorming and solving p r o b l e m s that c o m p e t i t o r s a r e still s u f f e r i n g . N o w y o u ' v e g o t it.
Now you know why I

(McLean, from page 5)
It is and always has been an American zeal
to be first in anything we do. and to win, and
to win, and to win.
Every time a football player goes to play
his trade he's got to play from the ground upfrom the soles of his feet right up to his head.
Every inch of him has to play. Some guys
play with their heads. That's OK. You've got
to be smart to be number one in any business. But more importantly, y o u ' v e g o t t o
play with y o u r heart, with every fiber of your
body. If vou're lucky enough to find a guy
with a lot of head and a lot of heart, he's never going to come, off the field second.
Running a football team is no different
than running any other kind of organizationan army, a political party or a business. The
principles are the same. The object is to
w i n - t o beat the other guy. Maybe that
sounds hard or cruel. I don't think it is. If is a
reality of life that men are competitive and
the most competitive games draw the most
competitive men. That's why they are there
- t o compete.
Tb know the rules and objectives when they get in the game. The object
is to win fairly, squarely, by the rules-butto
win.
And in truth, I've never known a man
worth his salt who in the long run, deep down
in his heart, didn't appreciate the grind, the
discipline. There is something in good men
that really vearns for discipline and the harsh
reality of head to head combat. I don't say
these things because I believe in the "brute"
nature of man or that men must be brutalized
to be combative^ I believe in God, and! believe in human decency. But I firmly believe
that any man's finest hour, the greatest fulfillment of all that he holds dear, is that moment
when he has worked his heart out in a good
cause and lies exhausted on the field of battle - victorious. -<- Vince Lombardi
So the question i s t h i s : D o y o u t a k e
1 0 0 % responsibility for everything in your
life? A n d if not - h o w can that c h a n g e ? P e r s o n a l p o w e r a n d F U L L c o n t r o l is j u s t a r o u n d
the corner, b u t O N L Y for the person w h o i s
willing t o accept responsibility (good a n d
bad) for E V E R Y result i n their life. F o r m e r
Dallas Cowboy's 2 x Super Bowl Winning
c o a c h J i m m y J o h n s o n s a i d it b e s t : "In my
life, I want s o m e of the credit a n d a l l of
the blame."
#END#

A l l I k n o w . . . . it a i n ' t t r a n s f e r r i n g b l i p s o f d a t a . It i s . . . . w a i t f o r
i t . . . . T A L K I N G t o o n e a n o t h e r ! A c t u a l l y b e i n g present i n h u m a n
conversation, a n d not t o b e lost in s o m e app o r techno g i z m o
that distracts being 'here' t o ' w h e r e ever.' Until next time. E n j o y
t h e s u m m e r until A l G o r e b l a m e s all t h e s u n s h i n e o n global
warming.
#END#

w h e n things aren't going well, I need to re-evaluate what I a m

Getting Into Canada's
"Top 1%"...
The Bar is Far LOWER
Than I Thought

way around) and so forth - we should feel pretty
good, shouldn't we? NO!
In order to get into the Top 1 % in the U.S.A.
you would have to be earning over $370,000 a
year! Heck, even in England - quite a 'socialistic'
country - you still have to be making close to
$225,000 to be in the top 1 %!
And then there is the distribution of where the
people are who earn $200K or more a year.
1. Ontario

I was watching the news the other night, and my attention was caught when they announced that an upcoming
segment was going to be about the widening gap between
the Top 1%" of income earners in Canada and everybody
else. I stuck around through a bunch of commercials and
crappy news to hear it...
Of course, there was a bunch of whining and pundits
pontificating about the 'widening gap' between the 'haves
and the have-nots' in Canada. I have to admit that I was
shocked to hear that 20% of Canada's population lives on
$15,000 a year or less!
But do you want to know what really surprised me and,
to be quite frank, pissed me off? It was how LOW the bar is
set to be one of the "Top 1%" of income earners in this
country! You only have to be making $201,000.00 a year to
be in the 'Top 1%" of income earners in Canada.
Now, don't get me wrong, $200K a year is a good
amount of money - but there is no way I thought that was
the benchmark for entering into the elite group of being one
of the top 1%!
To give you some perspective, in the United States - this
is a country which we have seen go through some pretty
tough economic times recently - being on the 'fiscal cliff, and that we have somewhat smugly looked at and felt financially superior to. I mean, with our dollar being at par (or
higher), with so many Canadians going South to buy up
properties, with Canadian businesses buying up American
enterprises (instead of it always seeming to be the other

2. Quebec
3. Alberta
4. British Columbia
I guess that makes sense
from a pure "population" point
of view. Ontario and Quebec
hold the lion's share of the
population of the country, so they should have
the highest number of "Top 1 percenters".
As you can tell, I was pretty shaken up by this
revelation. On one hand, I'm upset that the bar is
so low here, especially compared to the USA.
On the other hand, I'm encouraged. This information tells me that it is way easier than I ever
imagined to be in the 'Top 1%". It used to be that
I would encourage people to be a 'Top 20 percenter" (from the Pareto Principal - that 80% of
most country's wealth is held by 20% of the
population) - now I'm going to be telling everyone
to shoot for being a 'Top 1 percenter"...
Because, I don't know what it says for our
country as a whole, but if you really look at it,
getting into that 'elite club' isn't that big of a
stretch.

END.

Are YOU a Renegade Entrepreneur?
T h e r e is O N L Y t r u e s u c c e s s in life; t o be a b l e t o live a n d w o r k o n Y O U R o w n t e r m s . B e i n g a
" R e n e g a d e E n t r e p r e n e u r " is a l l a b o u t B E H A V I O U R ( n o t p h i l o s o p h y o r a t t i t u d e ) . H o w y o u
A C T o n a daily b a s i s . Renegade means contrarian - d e f y i n g n o r m a l , c o m m o n , o r d i n a r y ,
c u s t o m a r y b o u n d a r i e s w h e n it c o m e s t o y o u r p e r s o n a l a n d b u s i n e s s b e h a v i o u r a n d e x i s t e n c e . You work and live life on YOUR terms.
T h e U L T I M A T E g o a l o f a n y R e n e g a d e E n t r e p r e n e u r a n d B u s i n e s s O w n e r i s ' A u t o n o m y ' . Autonomy is as
follows: Doing what vou want, when vou want, where vou want, with whom vou want, at the price vou want on
the terms vou want. 1 0 0 % r e s p o n s i b i l i t y A N D a c c o u n t a b i l i t y f o r e v e r y r e s u l t in y o u r life g o o d a n d b a d . Y o u w a n t
the credit - a n d t h e b l a m e . Y o u w a n t a s m u c h control and, therefore, responsibility a s possible.

f o r s l e e p i n g a n d e a t i n g . A n o t h e r 1 0 % f o r life's o t h e r d u t i e s like p a p e r w o r k , p a y i n g t h e bills a n d s t a n d i n g in l i n e s .
T h a t b r i n g s u s a l l t o a n A V E R A G E life e x p e c t a n c y n u m b e r o f 54.5 years ( g i v e o r t a k e a f e w ) .

Tax Deductions for Online Marketers

T a x s e a s o n is h e r e ,
and for s o m e of y o u , it
m i g h t b e t h e first t i m e r e porting y o u r online income.
Yes, the C R A (and the I R S
in t h e U S ) d o e s e x p e c t y o u
to pay taxes on your online
e a r n i n g s . H o w e v e r , it's n o t
all d o o m a n d g l o o m ; j u s t
as y o u pay taxes o n inc o m e earned, y o u also get
tax relief o n business expenses. Knowing your expenses can reduce a tax
bill a n d m a y e v e n i n c r e a s e
a tax refund.
T h e first t h i n g y o u
s h o u l d d o is find y o u r s e l f a
good tax preparer, not
s o m e national, franchise
tax service. T h o s e places
w i t h a gorilla o u t s i d e , t o s s ing a n a r r o w s i g n , c a n n o t
advise you; they only rec o r d w h a t y o u tell t h e m . A
g o o d t a x p r e p a r e r w i l l sit
d o w n with y o u , talk with
you about your business,
and be able to advise you
on what deductions are
appropriate for your situat i o n . A lot o f l a w y e r s o f f e r
this service, as t h e y h a v e
a good understanding of
the relevant laws, and Attorney-Client Privilege also
protects y o u .
H e r e is a list o f s o m e o f
the things y o u might be
able to claim as business
expenses for your online
marketing.
1 . T h e b e s t d e d u c t i o n is
your tax preparer's fees.
N o t o n l y w i l l y o u get g r e a t
advice, but y o u can claim
the cost of that advice too.

Page 8

2 . T h e H o m e O f f i c e is
always a popular deduction; however, you need
to be careful about
claiming this. T h e C R A
has very set ideas on
w h a t they consider qualif i e s a s a h o m e office.
B a s i c a l l y , it h a s t o b e a n
a r e a o f y o u r h o m e t h a t is
exclusively used for your
business. S o the kitchen
table d o e s not count, but
a d e s k in t h e b a s e m e n t
c o u l d . If y o u d o qualify,
then you can also claim
part o f y o u r r e n t o r m o r t g a g e a n d utilities a s
business expenses.
3 . O n l i n e c h a r g e s relating t o y o u r i n c o m e . P a y Pal fees, Fiverrr fees,
basically a n y f e e s that
you had to pay to receive your income could
be considered as deductions.
4 . Stationery. Not just
the usual printer ink and
paper, but also business
cards, the fliers that y o u
left all o v e r t o w n a d v e r t i s i n g a C P A offer, e n v e lopes and s t a m p s for
t h o s e direct mail offers
you sent to generate
leads for offline clients.
5. All t h o s e W S O ' s ,
Clickbank ebooks, and
online webinars that y o u
paid for can be considered expenses too.
6. Website costs. Don't
forget hosting, domain
n a m e charges, graphics,
and plugins.
7. S E O Services.
Unlike Google, the C R A
does not care what services you used to rank

your sites,
only h o w
m u c h it c o s t
a n d t h a t it is
related to
generating
income.
8. Advertising costs:
Facebook
fees,
Adsense
fees, paying to h a v e your
link o n p o p u l a r Y o u T u b e
v i d e o s , s o l o a d s t o build
y o u r m a i l i n g list, b o u g h t
traffic v i a P P V s h o u l d all
be considered.
9. Website Content:
Yes, even those onedollar, 3 0 0 w o r d s , s p u n
to death articles, h a v e
value as an expense.
10. Online subscriptions
f o r i n f o r m a t i o n , i.e. S e o Moz, rank checking,
backlink analysis and
competitor information
should be noted d o w n .
T h e s e are just a few
ideas to get you started
thinking about possible
online marketing expenses that you can present to your tax preparer.
A g o o d t a x p r e p a r e r is
w o r t h t h e i r w e i g h t in
g o l d . T h i s is t h e i r e x p e r t i s e , s o u s e it. T h e y c a n
advise you best on which
e x p e n s e s are currently
a l l o w e d ( I R S d o e s like t o

change the rules) and
also w h a t percentage
of the expense can be
justified a s business
r e l a t e d . Y o u r cell
p h o n e is a l e g i t i m a t e
e x p e n s e , but y o u
might only be able to
really j u s t i f y 2 5 % a s
business use.
Marketing Gurus
often talk about
"leaving m o n e y o n the
table," referring to
missed income opportunities. However,
missed expenses can

a n s w e r is

can use to accelerate your

are self-employed, y o u don't

based on the results you
p r o d u c e . In s p i t e o f t h i s , I
w o u l d be willing to bet that

Scams and Bad Investments
T h i s a r t i c l e is g o i n g t o
be s o r t o f a c o n t i n u a t i o n
f r o m l a s t m o n t h ' s article o n
eBay scams, except w e
are going to be talking
about investment scams
and things to look a t before investing. Recently, I
have had a number of people a s k m y a d v i c e a b o u t
various investment opportunities, a n d I keep telling
people the s a m e things
over and over again, so I
thought I would just write
t h i s article a b o u t it. T h e
first t h i n g e v e r y o n e n e e d s
t o r e a l i z e is t h a t t h e r e a r e
scams and s c a m m e r s out
there, and you need to b e
a w a r e o f t h a t fact. Y o u
also need to be a w a r e that
a s c a m is d i f f e r e n t t h a n a n
investment that has gone
b a d , a n d t h a t n o t all i n v e s t ment losses are scams.
Investment scams can
be v e r y difficult t o s p o t a n d
defend against. E v e r y o n e
likes to h a v e s o m e protection w h e n they m a k e a n
i n v e s t m e n t . In m o s t c a s e s ,
that protection c o m e s in
the f o r m of security of
s o m e sort, w h e t h e r it is a
mortgage, a property,
o t h e r s e c u r i t y , o r all o f
them. People generally
think that the m o r e security
they get, the safer the inv e s t m e n t , a n d in a lot o f
c a s e s , t h a t is t r u e . H o w ever, y o u need to realize
t h a t it r e a l l y d o e s n o t m a t ter h o w m u c h security y o u
h a v e if y o u a r e d e a l i n g
with a s c a m m e r w h o is
determined to scam you.
O n t h e o t h e r h a n d , if y o u
are dealing with a trustworthy, h o n e s t p e r s o n , y o u
can have absolutely N O
s e c u r i t y b u t still h a v e a

very safe investment.
Everything depends on
the person you are dealing
with. I don't m e a n the
s a l e s m a n selling y o u the
investment, but the head
p e r s o n w h o is a c t u a l l y
making the decisions
a b o u t t h e i n v e s t m e n t . I tell
i n v e s t o r s t h a t t h a t is t h e
person you need to b e
dealing with, and the act u a l i n v e s t m e n t is s e c o n dary to the person, bec a u s e in reality y o u a r e
i n v e s t i n g in t h e p e r s o n a n d
N O T in t h e i n v e s t m e n t .
Y o u need to look at the
p e r s o n in c h a r g e a n d
m a k e a decision. D o you
trust this person, and d o
y o u think that this person
c a n m a k e m o n e y ? If y o u
don't think y o u can trust
the person, t h e n don't invest with him! T h e reasons
y o u h a v e for not trusting
t h e m are not very important. T h e y can e v e n b e
v e r y trivial r e a s o n s . T h e
reality is t h a t y o u r s u b c o n s c i o u s is t r y i n g t o tell y o u
t h a t s o m e t h i n g is w r o n g ,
s o l i s t e n t o it. A l s o if y o u
don't think the person can
m a k e m o n e y , then don't
invest with t h e m . Look at
their track record, and see
if t h e y h a v e m a d e m o n e y
in t h e p a s t . D o n ' t b e t u r n e d
o f f if y o u f i n d o u t t h a t t h e y
h a v e lost s o m e m o n e y i n
t h e p a s t . In reality, e v e r y
successful investor has
l o s t s o m e m o n e y in t h e
past. W h a t y o u n e e d t o
l o o k a t is t h e n e t result:
h a v e t h e y m a d e significantly m o r e m o n e y than
t h e y h a v e l o s t ? If a p e r s o n
tells y o u t h e y h a v e n e v e r
lost any m o n e y or never
m a d e a bad investment,

t h a t is a s u r e s i g n t o b e
worried. Also, try to find
o u t if t h e y k n o w w h a t
they are doing. This
s o u n d s really stupid t o
say, but successful people a r e s u c c e s s f u l b e cause they know what
they are doing. T h e y are
generally successful in
more than one area of
expertise because they
learn and research
things before they do
them. This again goes to
the point that the actual
i n v e s t m e n t is s e c o n d a r y
t o t h e p e r s o n . T h e right
p e r s o n in c h a r g e will
m a k e sure the investm e n t is profitable r e g a r d l e s s o f w h a t it is.
W h e n doing research, another thing
m a n y i n v e s t o r s d o is g e t
the advice of s o m e professionals, such as lawyers or accountants.
T h i s is g o o d t o d o , but
take their r e c o m m e n d a t i o n s w i t h a g r a i n o f salt.
T h e y a r e g o o d at telling
y o u specifics about a
particular investment,
but d o n ' t let t h e m m a k e
the decision for you. R e m e m b e r that their default
p o s i t i o n is t o N O T i n v e s t ,
b e c a u s e if t h e y s a y it i s
O K and the investment
goes bad, then they
c o u l d b e h e l d legally
liable f o r t h e a d v i c e t h e y
gave you. T h e y are N O T
investors, they are prac-

ticing p r o f e s s i o n a l s . I f
they were successful
investors you can be
pretty s u r e t h e y w o u l d
n o l o n g e r be p r a c t i c ing, or y o u w o u l d n o t
be able to afford t h e m .
Also remember that
many scammers know
y o u will b e c h e c k i n g
with these professiona l s , s o t h e y will a c t u ally s e t u p t h e i r s c a m s
so that accountants
a n d lawyers will look
favorably on the investment. Y o u would
probably be better off
to check with other
investors not connected with the investment you are looking
at. T h e y will g e n e r a l l y
give you a m o r e honest answer because
they do not have a
v e s t e d i n t e r e s t in it.
If y o u w o u l d like t o
find out m o r e about
what I recommend to
investors, please drop
m e an email and ask
m e to do s o m e m o r e
follow up articles about
it. If t h e r e is i n t e r e s t
then I m a y also talk
about s o m e of the bad
investments I have
made.

Negotiating Your Mortgage Renewal
Too
many people a r e n o t
a w a r e that
you can negotiate your mortgage renewal terms, and
blindly t a k e t h e first o f f e r
from their bank, which usually c o m e s in t h e m a i l .
T h i s is N O T in y o u r b e s t
i n t e r e s t . T h e b a n k is in t h e
business to m a k e money,
a n d l e n d i n g m o n e y is o n e o f
their prime sources of reven u e . A s a r e s u l t , t h e y m a k e it
very easy to r e n e w a mortgage for a n existing customer that has been making
payments o n a regular basis.
Y o u have already proven to
them that you are a good
source of income.
In t h i s m o n t h ' s article, I
will s h a r e w i t h y o u h o w e a s y
it is t o g e t t h e " B e s t " r a t e
and, at t h e end, s h a r e a n
example of the savings possible.
Jeff and I o w n multiple
p r o p e r t i e s , s o m e w i t h partners and s o m e on our own.
T h e majority of these properties h a v e mortgages (we
L O V E d e b t t h a t is p a i d f o r b y
our tenants and continues to
appreciate). This year, w e
have several mortgages
coming up for renewal.
E v e r y dollar w e s a v e by n e gotiating a lower interest rate
increases our C a s h F l o w . . .
a n d i n c r e a s e s o u r ability t o
finance future purchases as
well as increases cash flow
to our partners and to support o u r f i n a n c i a l l y f r e e lifestyle.
In t h e p a s t , w e e l e c t e d t o
choose longer term mortg a g e s (3 t o 5 y e a r ) a s w e l l
as variable rates. T h e r e are

Page 1 4

m a n y reasons for this,
s u c h a s the exit strategy
for each property, timing
of the U S A elections, the
e c o n o m y , etc.
T h i s y e a r , w e will
consider the s a m e factors a s well a s look at
w h a t w e b e l i e v e will b e
happening as a result of
the weakening U S econo m y . W e listen to advisors w h o have what w e
want and have been
w h e r e w e are at — a n d
then consider their advice and take action that
m a k e s sense to us.
W h i l e the majority of
C a n a d i a n s only purchase o n e or t w o houses
in t h e i r l i f e t i m e , t h e n e e d
to renew mortgages occ u r s t h r o u g h o u t t h e life
of the mortgage, so you
might as well learn h o w
to get the best deal. T h e
g o o d n e w s is t h a t it is a
similar process to w h a t I
h a v e w r i t t e n a b o u t in
past newsletters.
B a n k s m a k e it v e r y
easy to renew - easier
than qualifying for the
mortgage was! However,
an easy process does
n o t a l w a y s m e a n it is in
your best interest financially.
T h e normal process
f o r r e n e w a l is t h a t y o u
will r e c e i v e y o u r d o c u m e n t s in t h e m a i l f r o m
your bank. T h e docum e n t s will o f t e n h a v e a
variety of options to
choose from, such as
Fixed Rate vs Variable,
open or closed mortgage, and the term
length. Most times, the

rates they offer y o u are
the "Posted" rates,
which are never the
best deal. Often time,
this paperwork also includes a renewal fee
cost that m a y range in
price f r o m $ 7 5 to h u n dreds of dollars.
T h e paperwork enc o u r a g e s y o u to "select"
t h e o p t i o n s y o u like a n d
simply return the docum e n t s to the b a n k by
m a i l , o r t o call t h e m a n d
confirm your selection.
S o m e b a n k s will a l s o
give you a n 800 number
for their "Special"
r a t e s . . . . w h i c h will b e
slightly b e t t e r t h a n w h a t
they mailed y o u , but are
not usually the best
rates available. B a n k s
m a k e it e a s y f o r y o u t o
renew, so you do N O T
look around and see
w h a t e l s e is a v a i l a b l e .
Jeff and I discussed
our options and decided
w e w o u l d like a 1 y e a r
t e r m , closed rate. T h e
basis for our decision is
that although rates are
going to go up, t h e y will
n o t likely g o u p m u c h , if
at all, o v e r t h e next 12 18 m o n t h s , a n d w e a r e
considering selling our
p r i m a r y r e s i d e n c e in t h e

next year or two. Y o u
h a v e to m a k e the decis i o n o n w h a t is r i g h t f o r
your plans down the
road.
T h e other factor w a s
that the spread bet w e e n interest rates
w a s very low. During
the w e e k w e w e r e looking, a 1 y e a r rate w a s at
3 . 6 5 % ; 2 year at 3 . 5 5 % ;
3 y e a r at 3 . 9 5 % a n d a 5
y e a r at 4 . 2 4 % w h i c h
m e a n t a difference of
just .96 % over the 5
year span.
Historically speaking, w h e n interest rates
are so very close in
percentage over a long
p e r i o d o f t i m e , it is a
strong indication that
the banks do not bel i e v e t h a t r a t e s will b e
rising a n y t i m e s o o n ,
which means they

"May I Help You?"
Greetings, Canadian
Business Profiteers! This
m o n t h I w a n t t o tell y o u a
t r u e s t o r y . ( H e y , all m y s t o r i e s a r e t r u e . B u t t h i s o n e is
even truer!) I w a s doing a
radio interview (on blogt a l k r a d i o . c o m ) . M y first. A n d
I really e n j o y e d m y h o u r - l o n g
chat with business consultant/guru/master, Marilyn
Anderson, host of a s h o w
called "People, B o o k s and
Biz," w i t h ( o f c o u r s e ) M a r i l y n
Anderson. I r e m e m b e r that
t h e h i g h l i g h t o f t h e s h o w (for
m e ) w a s s h a r i n g a little s t o r y
about a men's w e a r store
that w a s the scene of o n e of
m y v e r y first b u s i n e s s t u r n arounds. A n d that (naturally)
reminded m e to share the
story with y o u . S o here goes.
First a search for
"unused opportunities."
This store w a s going under
— fast — during the Great
R e c e s s i o n o f t h e 1 9 8 0 ' s . It
h a d a n a l l - m a l e staff, w h o
h a d all t o l d m e (in a n initial
meeting) that they w e r e prep a r e d t o d o w h a t e v e r it t o o k
to survive. (Good. T h e stage
w a s set.) I c a m e into the
store on the morning w e
w e r e scheduled to start this
t u r n a r o u n d a d v e n t u r e . I did
m y usual tour with the owner
and then just observed—
looking for "unused opportunities."
A t n o o n , I called the staff
together and said,
" G e n t l e m e n , I've b e e n
watching the w a y you greet
customers w h e n they enter
the store — and I think w e
can start our t u r n a r o u n d just
by a p p r o a c h i n g v i s i t o r s differently." I w e n t o n , " W h e n
you approach a customer,
y o u all s a y , 'May I help
you?' B u t t h a t j u s t g e t s t h e

r e s p o n s e , 'No thanks,
I'm j u s t looking,'
or
w o r d s t o t h a t effect. A n d .
B e c a u s e y o u ' r e all s o tall
— and 'so' well dressed
— your greeting sounds
like w h a t you're really
a s k i n g is, 'Do you have
any money?' Or... 'Can
you afford to shop
here?" (I h a d t h e m
l a u g h i n g , but I w a s
deadly serious.)
"Instead of asking
that mechanical 'may I
help y o u ? ' question," I
said, "why not treat each
visitor to the store as a n
individual h u m a n being?
If a v i s i t o r l o o k s like
t h e y ' r e in a h u r r y , w h y
not m o v e quickly with
them, and say s o m e t h i n g like, 'Can I help
you find
something?'
O r . If s o m e o n e s a u n t e r s
in, w h y n o t s a y , 'Good
morning. If you're j u s t
browsing
please
make
yourself at home.
There's coffee,
juice,
tea and water on the
juice bar.... I'd be
happy to give you a
tour if you like, or,
please, j u s t make
yourself at home...' T h e n ,
just respond to however
they respond." T h e y
agreed. A n d they also
agreed that n o n e of
t h e m had ever experie n c e d t h a t k i n d o f visitorfriendly w e l c o m e to a
store. A n y store. Anywhere. Ever. T h e n w e
spent a f e w minutes role
-playing h o w they would
actually word these
greetings. A n d then they
w e n t back to work.
Now let's do the
numbers... T h e i r

"conversion ratio" of
v i s i t o r s t o s a l e s t h a t first
morning of our turnaround w a s just 2 0 %
(two sales for every ten
visitors). T h a t afternoon

— and without spending

anything else.

both "the power of intention," and "the power

m e a n s paying attention

w a y w e do things n o w

thing w e do - so that w e
c a u s e the effect that w e
"intend" to cause (which
m e a n s getting the sales
r e s u l t s w e n e e d by b e ing a b l e t o e n g a g e w i t h
customers so that w e
are able to totally u n cover a n d satisfy their
n e e d s ) . In t h i s c a s e ,
paying attention leads
to finding w a y s to w e l come and engage with
v i s i t o r s in a m a n n e r t h a t
m a k e them feel genu-

inely w e l c o m e and
cared about. T h i s creates a feeling of trust w h i c h in t u r n l e a d s t o
customers opening up
about what motivated
t h e m to c o m e into t h e
s t o r e in t h e first p l a c e .
This kind of thoughtful
interaction defuses
sales resistance, so

and tend to speak with
sales staff as "people"

" s a l e s m e n " (in t h e o l d ,

HIM!
Some of the lessons I wanted to share with you about selling premium priced products and services:
• Never sell a product or a service. S e l l t h e e x p e r i e n c e . S e l l t h e u n i q u e p e r s o n t h e y a r e a b o u t t o b e c o m e i n v o l v e d w i t h ( l i k e t h e l o b s t e r f i s h e r m a n a n d t h e c o l o r blind a r t i s t ) . S e l l t h e m o n Y O U - a n d t h e f e e l i n g Y O U
help t h e m achieve.
• Always be thinking about ways you can tie in your clients into a membership program. T h e y d o n o t
shop for other membership programs (mainly because f e w people provide them).
• Always think about unique ways to bundle higher end products and services to double, triple, quadruple your price points.
• Limit access to you. G i v e t h e m a c c e s s t h r o u g h t h i n g s like n e w s l e t t e r s , b u t d o n o t g i v e t h e m d i r e c t a c c e s s t o
y o u - a t l e a s t d o n ' t m a k e it t o o e a s y . M a k e t h e m g o t h r o u g h a f e w h o o p s t o g e t t o y o u directly - e v e n c h a r g e
t h e m f o r t h e ability t o d o s o . R a t h e r t h a n g i v i n g f r e e p r o p o s a l s a n d q u o t a t i o n s t o t h e m - c h a r g e f o r it. I m m e d i a t e l y , y o u b e c o m e d i f f e r e n t t h a n e v e r y o n e e l s e t h e y d e a l w i t h . E v e n if t h e y d e c i d e t o g o e l s e w h e r e , t h e y w i l l
a l w a y s r e m e m b e r y o u a s t h e o n e w h o didn't w a n t t o give things a w a y f o r free.
• Delay the sales cycle. M a k e it s e e m ( t o t h e m ) t h a t it t a k e s f o r e v e r t o g e t t h e d e a l d o n e w i t h y o u . H a v e a n
actual process in place to slow d o w n t h e sale. M a k e t h e m w o r k harder to spend their m o n e y .
• Give them a good story to tell their friends and family. H i g h n e t w o r t h b u y e r s a r e y o u r b e s t p l a c e f o r r e f e r rals - a n d t h e y l o v e t o r e f e r p e o p l e w h o h a v e a g o o d s t o r y t h e y c a n tell ( t h e c o l o r blind artist - t h e $ 3 , 0 0 0 l o b ster program).
• Bundling products and services - m a k e it s o t h e y h a v e t o b u y b u n d l e X i n o r d e r t o b e a b l e t o b u y b u n d l e Y .
• Re-think WHO you are selling to. Is t h e r e s o m e o n e in a b e t t e r i n c o m e b r a c k e t t h a t y o u c a n r e p a c k a g e a n d
sell t o ? ( O w e n did t h i s w i t h h i s a r t w o r k . R a t h e r t h a n s e l l i n g a r t w o r k - h e s e l l s c o r p o r a t e gifts a n d c o m p e t e s
with gold shirt promotional c o m p a n i e s - n o real competition).
S o m e o f t h e s e s t r a t e g i e s c a n e a s i l y triple o r q u a d r u p l e y o u r p r i c e s a n d r e v e n u e s . . . l e t a l o n e w h a t t h e y w i l l d o
f o r y o u r profits. Do the math - a simple doubling of price has a HUGE impact. 1 , 0 0 0 c u s t o m e r s b u y a t $ 1 0 0

C h a n g e initiatives w e r e generally m a n a g e d and
guided by people w h o s e specialty w a s reengineering companies and other organizations. T h e
c h o i c e w a s b e t w e e n t o t a l f a i l u r e a n d radical c h a n g e .

m e a n t the s a m e thing: u n e m p l o y m e n t . F r o m the pers p e c t i v e o f big b u s i n e s s a n d s m a l l b u s i n e s s a l i k e ,
c h a n g e w a s d e s i g n e d t o i n c r e a s e t h e t o t a l efficiency,
a n d it w o r k e d , f o r t h e m o s t part. A l l o f t h i s m e a n t a
new creation of value in t h e e c o n o m y . W h e t h e r by
breaking up companies, closing t h e loss making
o n e s , transferring o w n e r s h i p by w a y of e m p l o y e e
E S O P S , or sending jobs elsewhere.
A s G e o r g e F r i e d m a n w r o t e in J o h n M a u l d i n ' s
e c o n o m i c s n e w s l e t t e r 1 4 J a n u a r y 2 0 1 2 : As the permanent corporate jobs declined, more people were
starting over. Some of them were starting over every
few years as the agile corporation grew more efficient and needed fewer employees. That meant that
if they got new jobs, it would not be at the munificent
corporate pay rate, but at near entry-level rates in the
small companies that were now the new growth engine. As these companies failed, were bought or
shifted direction, they would lose their jobs and start
over again."
