As part of our due diligence, weekly we receive email
updates on rates from a few different mortgage brokers,
which include both "Posted" rates and "Best" rates, which
they have negotiated on behalf of their clientele. That
same week, the "Best" rates ranged from 2.69% to 2.94%
for a 5 year term, a spread of just a quarter of a point. Further indication that using a broker or negotiating on your
own can get you a much better deal.

So what's the bottom line? Using a carefully
constructed and creative Shock and Awe package that emphasizes your experience, credibility,
authority and, if possible, humour can be one of
the most effective ways by which to transform
yourself from just another brown cow to animpossible-not-to-be-noticed Purple Cow!

For some properties and for some people, this is the
right thing to do. It is important to think about your individual circumstances and the length of time you may be keeping the property before making a decision, however you
should still negotiate the very best rate you can.

Jeff and I called our bank and asked for their "Special"
Rates. They were higher than the Best rates indicated by
our research, and I asked if they could match these rates.
The answer was NO.

#END#

Using my philosophy of never taking a No from a person not in a position to give a YES, we made an appointment to visit our branch to negotiate on our own. Note: you
could also call a mortgage broker to do this for you, but it
does mean qualifying for a new mortgage and gathering all new paperwork, proof of income, NOA's, etc. and if
you have had a change in income or jobs, sometimes it is just smarter and more time efficient to stick with the
same bank and negotiate in person.
When we arrived at the branch we learned our former representative had left and a new person had taken
over. During the first few minutes, we "Built Affinity", we then made sure that we were dealing with a person that
could make decisions, a YES person. Next we "Gave them a reason to want tofinda Win~Win outcome"... we
were long term customers, we had multiple properties, we had done our homework, and we wanted to work with
them further into the future. We then asked if they would match the rates we had researched as the "Best" rates.
Their answer was YES ... just by us asking.
I want to give you a quick example of how much money the above process can save you. The example below
is based on a $300,000 mortgage, with a 5 year term, over a 25 year amortization and a monthly payment.
A posted interest rate of 4.24% - which was the bank's posted rate - would mean that you would pay $59,265
in the first 5 years on interest
while a negotiated rate of 2.94% - which was the BEST rate indicated by my
research - with the exact same terms would mean that you would pay just $40,748 in the first 5 years. A SAVINGS of $18,517 .... to your benefit.
Additionally - simply indicate that you do not wish to pay a renewal fee ... as some banks will also automatically charge you a Renewal Fee, and watch for any fees on any document you sign. Just be pleasant and ask
them to waive the fees. Jeff and I paid a renewal fee back in the 1980's before we understood that they could be
waived. Since that time, EVERY renewal fee has been waived, just by asking. More money in your pocket - or
perhaps to go out to dinner and celebrate the best mortgage rate you negotiated!
Feel free to contact me if there is anything I can do to help you out or if you have topic you would like me to
write about in the future! I LOVE my Life!
*END*

"It's the Niche or the Ditch!"
Greetings Canadian Business Profiteers! This month,
I want to share w h a t m a y be
the best example of niche
marketing I have ever seen.
O u r client, c o l l e a g u e a n d
friend, J i m T a l l m a n , w h o operates North Pacific Training
a n d P e r f o r m a n c e Inc., h a d a
p r o b l e m . If his c o m p a n y c o n tinued to accept every kind
of industrial training contract
that c a m e along, they would
forever be o n a learning
c u r v e — a n d a l w a y s a t risk o f
making mistakes—because
they don't k n o w a specific
client's i n d u s t r y w e l l e n o u g h .
N o w h i s p r o b l e m is s o l v e d .
North Pacific n o w w o r k s only
with clients w h o m e e t a very
specific criteria. T h e y m u s t
b e in t h e m i n i n g o r h e a v y
industry b u s i n e s s — b e c a u s e
that's w h e r e Jim and c o m p a n y identified t h e g r e a t e s t
need/payoff for their services, and their highest level
of experience and competence. Perfect.
In p a r t i c u l a r , J i m t a r g e t s
m i n e s in C a n a d a a n d a f e w
other jurisdictions w h e r e federal law n o w requires that
p e r s o n n e l b e t r a i n e d in m i n e
safety—or the mine manager
c a n a c t u a l l y f a c e j a i l t i m e if
s o m e o n e is h u r t o r killed i n
an accident. Ouch.
Wait. I can hear you
t h i n k i n g , " G e e if y o u n a r r o w
your target market to that
extent, won't y o u have a
problem getting e n o u g h
work?" Good question. But
J i m c o m b i n e s his t a r g e t m a r keting with a n aggressive
marketing campaign that
educates mine managers
(and h e a v y industry operators) about the risks they
f a c e if t r a i n i n g isn't d o n e
t h o r o u g h l y — a n d if a p a p e r

trail isn't c r e a t e d t h a t c a n
p r o v e (in a c o u r t o f l a w )
that thorough training
w a s conducted. And
word among mine mana g e r s h a s s p r e a d like
wildfire.
L o o k at the payback...
S i t d o w n f o r t h i s part.
O n e year ago, North
Pacific consisted of exactly t w o p e o p l e : J i m
and his partner D a n
McFaull. Today, they
h a v e a t o t a l o f 16 c o n tract trainers working
w i t h t h e m o n p r o j e c t s all
across Canada (and the
US), and the number of
clients grows (almost)
weekly.
I love this story because m y mantra for
y e a r s h a s b e e n , "It's t h e
n i c h e o r t h e ditch," a n d
I'm s u r e I've s h a r e d it
w i t h y o u h e r e at C a n a dian Business Profits
(one way or another).

y o u w i t h a r e a l call t o
a c t i o n : If y o u h a v e n ' t
already done so (and so
few business owners
have), w h y not take
s o m e t i m e t o identify a
w a y t o g e n u i n e l y differentiate yourself from
everyone else w h o does

what you do?

Tallman. You

With great reluctance, he agreed. From his perspective, what a great opportunity this would be for him. In exchange for just one day of attendance at a
"cult" event, a lifetime of no nagging on a similar subject and the opportunity to

Long story short - Jeff attended and we had an AMAZING time. Jeff was the
first one up in the morning for the next two days. We met many people who
later became our friends AND we shifted our context about money and mindset
so quickly and so dramatically that our entire wodd changed almost overnight.
Within 2 years, we were able to grow our real estate portfolio and build excellent JV relationships. The best part was that Jeff was able to quit his full time
job; he hated the commute. I elected to stay working part time, because we enjoy the benefits of what I do. I LOVE my Life!

T h e

P o w e r

o f P o s i t i o n i n g

I'm writing this article from beautiful PERTH, Ontario,
where the family and I are having a quick stop-over during
our Ontario/Quebec 3 Way Pay Day Tour. Perth is where
our good friend, and Canadian Business Profits contributing
writer, Michael McLean is based.
While here and chatting with Michael, I am reminded of
the power of POSITIONING - as Michael is a master at it,
and I've learned a lot from him about it.

One of the things he does
that absolutely drives his stodgy
competitors crazy is that he offers a FREE pizza with every
quote! What does pizza have to
do with getting a quote on insurance? Absolutely nothing! But it
creates curiosity, gives him a
very unique selling proposition,
and gives people a reason to
call for a quote with him rather
than his competition.

D a v e D u b e a u

_

P u b l i s h e r

This is why, in a town of 6,000 people, his
company has over 8,000 clients!

There are probably a lot of different
definitions of "Positioning", but for me it
i
boils down to putting your flag in the
ground and saying, "This is who I am
and what I stand for". In Michael's
case, it is with his small-town familyowned Insurance agency - and how he
positions himself as the 'fun', good guy
to work with versus the faceless corporate competition.

A i lI '

He took over from his father back in
2003 and has since more than tripled
the size of the business (after it had
already been around for 30 years). He
applied his amazing marketing skills to
it and really rocked his local industry
'norms' with outside-the-box direct response newsletters,
postcards, tv ads, flyers, you-name it.
He is loud, proud and absolutely shameless when it
comes to self-promotion and getting the word out about
himself and his agency. That is the key to his success. He
goes head-to-head against not only the GEICO's of the
worid, but also the other, larger local agencies in town (or
they were larger until he ate into their market share big
time).

^ With my old language training
^usiness in Costa Rica, I did
something like that myself. I came
up with an amazing guarantee
that became my unique selling
proposition and justification for
about a 50% premium over my
competition's pricing. This confree,
fizz*, founded them so much that it took
vArtt Your over 7 years for anyone to figure
it out and start copying me! Talk
|2*\surA>nei6? about a major head start!
yes!
' '• . '
So I suggest that you take a
page from Michael and really look
at what your positioning is. Then look at what it
could and should be in order to really make
things happen for you and your business. This
probably means taking a big leap out of your
comfort zone - and doing something that you,
your family and your employees are going to find
'uncomfortable'. Go ahead and do it anyway! Like
I always say, "If you want to get extraordinary
results you have to do extraordinary things."
END.

I started painting h o u s e s for
s o m e s i d e i n c o m e . It w a s e a s y
w o r k - and the guy I w a s painting for paid m e quite w e l l , for t h e
time. Don owned around 30
properties in C a l g a r y , w h e r e I
live... a n d m y job w a s to g o f r o m
property to property after school and o n w e e k e n d s to
s p r u c e t h e m up w i t h a c o a t of paint.
A t t h e t i m e , it w a s g r e a t . O u t i n t h e s u n a l l d a y . . .
making good coin... and no o n e watching over m e . (Don
usually s h o w e d up at the start of the job t h e n the end of
it, w i t h i n s t r u c t i o n s f o r t h e n e x t o n e . ) L i t t l e d i d I k n o w
t h a t t h i s little " s i d e j o b " w o u l d t u r n i n t o m y f i r s t s o l d e n t r e p r e n e u r i a l e x p e r i e n c e . . . and one of the BEST sales
lessons I ever had the privilege to learn.
After painting h o u s e s for the year after graduation
and learning that being the painter wasn't m y lifetime
a s p i r a t i o n . . . I d e c i d e d s c h o o l w a s n ' t all t h a t b a d . S o I
s q u e a k e d in w i t h m y d i s m a l g r a d e s i n t o t h e U n i v e r s i t y o f
Calgary Bachelor of C o m m e r c e program.
B e t w e e n parties, I found myself actually needing t o
e a t ( s o m e t h i n g o t h e r t h a n barley). S o off I w e n t in
s e a r c h o f a j o b - u g l y j o b s t o o . Then I heard about this
company called College Pro Painters. P a i n t i n g - I
k n o w h o w to do that! Off I w e n t to their office to s e e
w h a t this w a s all about. T h e r e I m e t w i t h t h e g e n e r a l
m a n a g e r to see w h a t they had to offer m e .
O W N A F R A N C H I S E ? I wasn't thinking along those
lines at first - but m y d r e a m s o f m e g a riches f r o m m y
n e w franchise glittered in m y e y e s - s o I signed up and
became a College Pro Painters Franchise Owner.
It was the smartest thing I had ever done! F o r t u nately, I didn't need to learn m u c h about painting—they
e v e n a s k e d m e to teach their n e w painters h o w to paint
properly. B u t I did n e e d t o learn m o r e a b o u t m a n a g e m e n t and door to door sales.
O n e of the w a y s w e got painting contracts w a s door

M A R K E T INFORMANT WITH BRYAN DAR SANTOS

Time for LIBOR-ation

C o n f i d e n c e in t h e f i n a n c i a l s e c t o r is a g a i n i n
danger of shattering
t h a n k s to a scandal that
h a s people questioning if
any bank can ever be
t r u s t e d . It h a s b e e n d i s covered that manipulation
is m o r e t h a n l i k e l y in s e t ting the L o n d o n InterBank Overnight Rate, or
L I B O R , by several m a j o r
financial institutions. W h a t
is L I B O R , w h a t h a p p e n e d , a n d h o w d o e s it
affect u s ?
L I B O R is t h e a v e r a g e
rate to which leading
b a n k s in L o n d o n w o u l d
lend to each other. L I B O R
is u s e d by n a tions across
the globe as
the primary
b e n c h m a r k to
set short-term
interest rates,
including c o n sumer loans
and savings
rates. B a n k s
are expected
to truthfully disclose their
estimated inter-bank lending rate. E v i d e n c e , h o w ever, has surfaced that
individuals at the b a n k s
w e r e fixing their interbank rate to benefit inhouse traders. Further
investigation h a s indicated that rate fixing h a s
gone beyond a few rogue
employees. Such behaviour m a y h a v e been e n dorsed, condoned or e n couraged by the b a n k s to
suit their o w n interests.
T h e a s s u m e d ramifica-

Page 6

tions are staggering. T h e savings rate of your account or
lending rate of your loan could
be set, based o n a b e n c h m a r k
w h o s e v a l u e is d e t e r m i n e d b y
reported rates which w e r e
f i x e d in o r d e r f o r a t r a d e r t o
m a k e m o r e m o n e y or for a
bank to gain a benefit. C a n a d i ans have reason to be
pissed...or do they?
The average consumer,
however, m a y not have much
to complain about. Firstly, t h e
L I B O R is a n a v e r a g e o f i n t e r bank rates from several financ i a l i n s t i t u t i o n s . E v e n if a f e w
b a n k s fixed their rates, the
overall impact to L I B O R would
be relatively minimal.
B a n k s o f t e n did not k n o w
w h a t their inter-bank rate
would be and, hence, submitted their best estimate.
Thusly, the LIBOR
figure s e r v e s m o r e a s
a n indicator of interbank rates rather than
an accurate measure.
Banks should be considering this w h e n setting s h o r t - t e r m interest
rates.

f e w basis points or hundredths of a percent. For
traders of investments based o n the inter-bank
rate, a s m a l l c h a n g e a m o u n t s to significant profits a n d l o s s e s d u e t o t h e s h e e r v o l u m e o f w h a t
they trade. For m o s t consumers, paying an extra basis point for a loan or receiving a basis
point less for savings wouldn't a m o u n t to a
m e a n i n g f u l a m o u n t . In fact, m a n y s h o r t t e r m
fixed i n c o m e products v a r y by m o r e t h a n 10 basis points, yet m a n y c o n s u m e r s are fine with the
lower savings rate or higher loan rate for a n u m b e r o f r e a s o n s . T h i s s h o w s t h a t s u c h s m a l l differences are acceptable and possibly w a s used
a s justification by t h o s e w h o perpetrated t h e
scandal.
O f c o u r s e , i t is n o t t o s a y t h a t it i s o k f o r
banks to manipulate rates or that w e should acc e p t a s l i g h t l y h i g h e r i n t e r e s t t h a n w e s h o u l d . It
i s s i m p l y t h a t t h e d e g r e e i s n o t a s d a m n i n g a s it
may sound.
It s h o u l d a l s o b e p o i n t e d o u t t h a t n o t e v e r y o n e s u f f e r s f o r m rate fixing. In fact, interest rates
are a z e r o s u m g a m e , so for every dollar that
s o m e o n e lost, a n o t h e r p e r s o n g a i n e d . C o n s i d e r
that, d u e to rate fixing, interest rates are set to a
lower a m o u n t by 10 basis points or 0 . 1 0 % . C o n s u m e r s a r e r e c e i v i n g 0 . 1 0 % l e s s in t h e i r b a n k
accounts, but those w h o are borrowing are paying 0 . 1 0 % less.
T h e o n e s w h o h a v e been really hurt are
those o n the other end of the L I B O R - b a s e d investment trades and those w h o have invested
or borrowed large s u m s of m o n e y over the short
term. W h i l e people have been wronged and
such practices should be stopped, the overall
i m p a c t t o t h e m a r k e t s s h o u l d b e m o o t . In fact,
a s of t h e writing of this article, t h e investor c o m munity has already found another issue to dwell
over.

W h e n s p e a k i n g w i t h clients about increasing i n come, author Jay Abraham
s i m p l i f i e d it p r e t t y w e l l . Y o u
s h o u l d all u s e this in y o u r

as in measuring your results.
H e identified three w a y s t o

1.

Measuring and increasing t h e n u m b e r o f a c t u a l
sales transactions (lead
generation).

2.

transaction (upsell o r
price increase).

3.

the s a m e customers
(asking, following up a n d
following through, customer service and special o f f e r s ) .

T o m e , t h e key word in
w h a t Jay A b r a h a m says is

where w e are to where w e

p e o p l e . In difficult t i m e s ,

protection. M a n y e v e n take
on a siege mentality (a state

or isolated) in their w o r k ,
their relationships, around
m o n e y a n d in the c o m m u n i ty. T h i s is t h e e x a c t o p p o s i t e
of what you should do.

w h o k n o w h o w t o pull t e a m s

together, m a x i m i z e resources, create energy

by predators. W h o d o
you surround yourself

you generating leads

H o w are you

are you
measuring

you are attempting t o

get rid o f all o f t h e u n healthy f o o d in y o u r
h o u s e . It i s e a s i e r t o
stay the course w h e n
you have to go to the

t h e c u p b o a r d . If y o u w a n t
to be productive a t work,

on your phone, and for

o p e n y o u r e - m a i l . If y o u

The Bottom Line.
E v e n t s like the O l y m p i c s

p e r s o n a l life i s t o t a k e a c tion. T h e place m o s t people fail is in t h e transition

Change Your Mind!
This
month,
w e look
at pet
peeves.
Things
our customers
do that
irk u s , a n n o y u s , f r u s t r a t e u s ,
anger us - and the ways w e
react to t h e m - which
(unintentionally) "always"
w o r k against us. In other
words, we're going to look at
h o w w e c a n turn gripes into
grins.
Here's a n example. This
morning I stopped at a service station that h a s a huge
sign that reads, " W a s h r o o m s
for c u s t o m e r u s e only." Just
for fun, I asked the m a n a g e r
f t h e fact that I'm a c u s t o m e r
today m e a n s that I can c o m e
and use the washroom tomorrow (without making a n other purchase)? H e looked
puzzled a n d didn't a n s w e r
r i g h t a w a y . S o I left h i m . . .
just standing there, thinking
a b o u t m y q u e s t i o n . A n d I'd
like y o u to d o t h e s a m e .
Obviously, this kind of
signage - e v e n t h o u g h it's
well intended (to keep o u t
t h e n o n - p a y i n g riff r a f f ) - i s a
turn-off for good customers
w h o just want to use the
w a s h r o o m o n a g i v e n visit.
T h e q u e s t i o n is, h o w d o
w e turn a gripe like this into
a grin? W e l l , t h e incident
reminded m e of one of m y
favorite stories, s o curl u p
a n d listen in.
I w a s speaking at a con. ference of Shell Oil dealers,
a number of years back, o n
o n e o f m y favorite topics:
" H o w to deliver service that
goes the mile beyond the

extra mile - w h e r e there
is n o c o m p e t i t o r traffic."
There were hundreds of
p e o p l e in t h e r o o m , s o t o
personalize the presentation, I did a "roving
mike" walk-through of
the audience - stopping
here and there to ask
people w h a t their pet
p e e v e s w e r e w h e n it
c a m e to customer service.
O n e d e a l e r said, "I
h a t e it w h e n n o n customers ask for
change for the laundrom a t next door," a n d added that h e h a d a huge
sign in h i s s a l e s r o o m
w i n d o w that read, " W e
don't m a k e change for
the laundromat." A m a z ingly (to m e ) w h e n h e
shared this gripe, h e a d s
n o d d e d all a r o u n d . "But,"
I asked h i m , "don't y o u
worry about turning good

" W h a t if y o u e l i m i n a t ed the annoyance," I
asked, "by stocking up
on change for the laundromat, instead o f giving
people a negative first
impression of your business? Y o u go to the

w h y not just be prepared
- a n d d o it w i t h a g r e a t
big s m i l e ? "
"All you'd h a v e to
do," I said, "is c h a n g e
your mind!"
I threw t h e idea o u t to
the whole audience for
comments. Sure, there's
a risk, w e all a g r e e d .
Heck, he could be

s w a m p e d by people
w h o just want change
and don't buy a thing.
B u t is t h a t really g o i n g
to happen? Probably
not.
N o w l e t m e tell y o u
w h a t actually did happen. I stressed the benefit t o h i m , personally,
of turning this gripe into
a g r i n ( h e y , if y o u c a n
d o it w i t h o n e t h i n g y o u
c a n d o it w i t h m a n y ) , s o

And I thought nothi n g m o r e o f it u n t i l I g o t

believe w h a t happened," h e said. " W h e n
I got home, w e changed
the sign to read, " Y e s ,

R V c a r a v a n , a n d I'm

N o w y o u r call t o action.
I urge everyone I work
with, including y o u loyal
Canadian Business
P r o f i t s r e a d e r s , t o list

around.

Three useful tips for preventing common accidents
D o n ' t leave a n y t h i n g t o chance w h e r e w o r k p l a c e safety
is c o n c e r n e d . I t ' s a n a l l - e n c o m p a s s i n g c h a l l e n g e , b u t
here are three solid tips t o start w i t h today:
• K e e p y o u r floors clear o f all cords and w i r e s . L o t s o f
office i n j u r i e s are due t o people's t r i p p i n g over electrical c o r d s t h a t t h e y d i d n ' t see w h i l e w a l k i n g t h r o u g h t h e
workplace.
• Secure a l l desk and filing cabinets t o t h e floors o r w a l l .
Y o u don't w a n t t h e m to tip over i f they become overloaded o r unbalanced, o r topple i n the event o f a s t o r m
or earthquake.
• K e e p storage areas clean. Shelves s h o u l d b e o r g a n i z e d
and o r d e r l y . D o n ' t leave boxes o r e q u i p - m e n t o n the
floor. Stored items should b e clearly labeled so e m p l o y ees c a n f i n d w h a t t h e y ' r e l o o k i n g f o r e a s i l y , w i t h o u t
having t o m o v e things a r o u n d o r c l u t t e r u p the area.

W h y s e e k t h e last penny for 'profit'....and hurt t h e
goodwill that y o u could gain by e n g a g i n g t h e locals.
O u r w e e k l y parked truck...filled... with aging loaves
of bread... b e c a m e T H E m a g n e t f o r t h e local population t o gravitate to t h e t o w n square. Scarcity a n d
poverty b e c a m e o u r leverage... by doing good.
Foregoing t h e credit o f only $ 1 0 0 per w e e k a n d
feeding families, led to o u r business not being boycotted a n d giving t h e business a trading m o n o p o l y
for about five w e e k s . T h a t w a s priceless! T h e w o r d
soon got out, a n d o u r regular e n g a g e m e n t with t h e
p o p u l a c e . . . g a v e i m m e n s e visibility f o r t h e y o u n g e r
A K 4 7 g u n toters. I also...soon... discovered that
t h e r e w a s v e r y little d i f f e r e n c e b e t w e e n t h e p o w e r o f
the gun...and t h e power of bread.
T h e first h a d t h e potentiality o f coercion...like
government fostered laws of exclusion and taxation.
T h e latter s e r v e d society t h r o u g h m e a n i n g f u l civilian
e n g a g e m e n t a n d t h e r e b y c r e a t i n g t h e repository of
the public confidence.
T h e moral of the Cashbuild and Trador Case
s t u d i e s , f o r p o s t e r i t y . . . make money by doing good!
W h y and h o w else would y o u matter? Physical cust o m e r e n g a g e m e n t , v i a local c o m m u n i t i e s b e c a m e
our mantra. H o w else does o n e gain loyal c u s t o m e r s ? M y v i e w is s i m p l e . . . y o u e n g a g e by sight, t o u c h ,
f e e l i n g a n d p a r t i c i p a t i n g i n real conversation....eye
ball t o e y e ball.
N O T advertising... 'socially' t o c u s t o m e r s in B e i j i n g f o r y o u r s u s h i b u s i n e s s in L e a f r a p i d s ! N o o n e
actually cares....and t h e n u m b e r o f Twit-hits don't
matter a darn thing...except to the P H D designers of
'social' technology.
So...until next time...please r e m e m b e r the followi n g : T o o p e r a t e a n authentic b u s i n e s s . . . i t i s b e s t t o
ensure that the V A L U E of your services A L W A Y S
e x c e e d s t h e P R I C E t h e ' l o c a l ' c u s t o m e r is w i l l i n g t o
pay!!! A n d ? T h a t y o u c a n e n g a g e h i m o r her.

(Elliott, from page 7)

Consequently, I was able to easily replace the
home maintenance company with a friend of mine who
cuts lawns on the side and does excellent work (there
is plenty of work available for people who can show up
and get the job done right). The other two 'consultants'
are now DEAD TO ME, and I quickly discovered they
need not be replaced.

item, when he could use exactly the same resources to promote a franchise that one of my clients is selling, for a commission of $6,000. He honestly couldn't answer my question, and he continued to work hard to earn $100 per sale. It's illogical,
yet that is precisely what most of us do. When we
remind ourselves that the purpose of business is to
earn the maximum net profit with the least time,
money, risk, and other costs, then delegation and
leverage become the natural choices.

A VERY successful businessman and Team Owner
of a hockey club I once coached told me the secret to
his PHENOMENAL business success was simple:
"Demand,
Demand,
D e m a n d . . . " I recognize this to
be MORE true each and every day. In the times we are
presently living, I think it is the difference between winning and losing. Just about everywhere you look, there
is some form of "Sales PREVENTION"
in place.
Scripts not being used, mail not being delivered, awful
customer service, no follow up, no consistent communication with clients & prospects - the list is endless.
Chet Holmes had a great line in his book 'The Ultimate
Marketing Machine': "They Will Respect
What YOU
Inspect."

Finally, when embarking on the exciting journey
of delegation and leverage, get the right guidance.
Do it wrong, and you can get hurt. Do it right, and
you'll leave your competition choking in your dust
and wondering what your secret is.
#END#

Hall of Fame football coach Bill Parcells based his
legendary coaching success on one basic but powerful
concept: "/ d o n ' t b e l i e v e i n incumbents
anywhere.
I
can only g o on what I s e e . If I don't like what I see, I
will make c h a n g e s . "
Renegade POWER Question: Take a look around
your world. What do you see? Are there things (and
people) you see that you do NOT like? Are some
changes and improvements in order? Do YOU need to
demand MORE and raise your level of expectations so
you can get to the NEXT level?
Also, "With Most Everyone
Demanding
LESS In
Their Business
- How Can We Demand
MORE And
Provide
The Ultimate
WOW Experience
For Our
Clients & Prospects?"
A few ideas that come to mind
- using VIDEO to better communicate, answering our
phones LIVE 24-Hours Per day (7 Days A week), recording all phone calls and reviewing to make sure
phone scripts are being used, adding an "On-Hold"
Recording to your phone system, committing to creating a 'Personality Driven' MONTHLY client newsletter,
collecting all client and prospect email (and consistently communicating), making ALL agency team production 'public' (automatically increases performance),
designing multiple NICHE target markets, designing a
TEAM production reward program, etc...
Marketing Legend Dan Kennedy said it best: "If I
wake up 2 nights in a row thinking
about you a n d
we are not sleeping
t o g e t h e r - You g o t to g o . "
